| `form` | `chibi`, `teen`, `adult` | `adult` | body form: heights and head share (section 4.3) |
| `build` | `slim`, `standard`, `large` | `standard` | base body build, widths only (section 4.3) |
| `scale` | whole % 85-110 | 100 | `generic.scale`; hitbox capped at 1.98 blocks |
| `palette` | map slot to colour | archetype default | slots: `skin`, `skin_shade`, `hair`, `hair_accent`, `eyes`, `primary`, `secondary`, `accent`, `trim`, `metal`, `leather`, `glow`; every part colours from slots |
| `parts` | list of 4-24 `{ part, color, params, pattern, pattern_colors, mirror, phase }` | archetype default | parts library entries (section 13.3); `phase` 2 or 3 makes it a `p2_`/`p3_` part |
| `face` | object | archetype and gender | the face system (13.5): `eyes` shape family, `width`, `spacing`, `iris` (and `iris2`), `iris_style`, `iris_width`, `pupil`, `highlights`, `lashes` 0-3, `flick`, `lower_lash`, `brows`, `mouth`, `fang`, `blush` (bool or a style), `marks` (freckles, beauty mark, scar, face paint, stubble, bandage; per side), `glasses`, `glasses_color`, `eyepatch`, `closed`, `overrides` (mood to frame substitutions) |
| `weapon` | object | style default | `prop` (a part id in `weapons/`), `hand` (`right`, `left`, `both`, `pair`), `sheathed` (`hip`, `back`, `none`), `glow` |
| `phases` | list | none | per phase: `show`, `hide` (bone or part ids), `palette` changes (a `_p2.png` is painted when any), `eyes_glow` |
| `glow` | list | none | regions painted into the glow mask (eyes in a phase, runes, a weapon edge, foxfire) |
| `markings` | list | none | body markings (stripes, scales, runes, seams) by slot and pattern |

### 12.6 `animations` (source only)

| Field | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `style` | one of 16 style ids | from the gear and `basic_attack` | the style library the hero falls back to |
| `blink` | `nervous`, `normal`, `calm`, `sleepy` | from temperament | blink profile (section 4.6) |
| `idle_quirks` | list of 0-3 primitive ids | from archetype | `personal.1-3` (ear flick, tail sway, paw lick, sword polish, page turn, yawn, hum...) |
| `signatures` | map animation name to recipe | required for each move, the ultimate, `phase.2`/`phase.3`, `intro` | recipes (section 13.6); a missing one falls back to a generated default from the move's form and class, with a NOTE |
| `overrides` | map | none | recipes replacing a library animation for this hero (its own `attack.1`, `victory`, `work.cook`...) |
| `source` | `generated`, `manual` | `generated` | `manual`: the hero's animation file was polished in Blockbench; the generator keeps it and reads `frames` from its markers |

### 12.7 `moves`

A move is a v1 ability (v1 §10: `name`, `form` and form fields, `effects`, `look`, `line`, `use_when`, `cooldown`,
`charges`, `energy_gain`; a v1 `unlock_level`, here or on the ultimate or a passive, is read and ignored with a NOTE,
because everything is there from level 1: section 8) plus:

| Field | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | id 2-24 | required | unique in the file; animation `move.<id>` |
| `class` | `light`, `heavy`, `area`, `ultimate` | from the form | sets the minimum wind-up and recovery (6.4) |
| `frames` | `{ windup, active, recovery }` seconds | generated from the animation markers | authored only with `animations.source: manual`; clamped to the class minimums |
| `band` | list of `close`, `mid`, `far` | from form and range | where the selector likes it (6.3) |
| `weight` | 0-10 | 5 | selector weight; 0 = only as a followup or reaction answer (a NOTE if nothing leads to it) |
| `phases` | list of 1-3 | all | the phases it belongs to (also settable from `boss.phases[].moves`) |
| `prefer_when` | condition | none | doubles the score when true |
| `followups` | list of `{ move, chance, max }` or move ids | none | strings (6.3); `chance` 1-100 (100), `max` 1-3 only on a repeatable move's own repeat |
| `repeatable`, `breaks_guard`, `unblockable`, `super_armor` | booleans | false | 6.4, 6.9 |
| `channel` | `{ interrupt }` % 5-30 | none | breakable by that share of max health in damage (weak point `channel`) |
| `telegraph` | `{ shape, color }` | from form | decal override (`disc`, `strip`, `fan`, `ring`, `landing`, `impacts`, `none` for light moves) |
| `per_shot` | list of effect lists | none | `barrage` only: each shot cycles to the next list |

### 12.8 `ultimate`

v1 ultimate (energy 100, `charge_rate`, minimum gap 20-60 s) plus move fields; class `ultimate`. `awakened`: a second
ultimate with the same shape, there from level 1 beside the first (the first draft unlocked it at level 25 to replace
the first; changed with the owner's answer of 2026-11-12, section 8). The two share the energy bar and the minimum
gap, and the selector picks between them by weight, band and `prefer_when`, as it does moves.

### 12.9 `states`

0-3 named states generalising v1's empowered state (v1 §18): a stance, a transformation, a rage. Fields: `name`,
`description`, `stats` (1-4, ±30%), `on_hit` (1-2 effects), `particle`, `color`, `size` (100-110), `skills_locked`,
`moves` (a replacement move list while in the state), `show`/`hide` (bones), `face` (a mood held while in the state),
`line`. Entered by `empower` with `"state": "<name>"`, by a phase (`boss.phases[].states`) or by a passive; the
condition `self_state` reads it. The animation `state.<name>` plays on entering.

### 12.10 `boss`

| Field | Type | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `health_scale` | whole % 200-600 | by rarity, +50 for leaders (6.2) | boss health multiplier; the role's boss factor (12.4) applies on top |
| `max_hit_share` | whole % 5-25 | 12 | per-hit cap on one hit from any attacker, player or NPC (6.2) |
| `poise` | 0 or 50-200 | by role | 0 only with another weakness |
| `phases` | 1-3 of `{ at, moves, states, adds, stats, transition }` | 2 or 3 by rarity | `at`: health share where it begins (first 100); `transition`: `{ seconds 0.8-2, line, shockwave {radius, strength}, effects, face }`; an `unlock_level` is read and ignored with a NOTE (every phase from level 1: section 8) |
| `reactions` | 0-8 of `{ when, move, chance, cooldown }` | none | 6.8 |
| `weak_points` | 1-4 | by archetype | 6.9 |
| `enrage` | `{ after, triggers, line }` | 180/240 s, none | 6.10 |
| `adds` | list of `{ id, count }` | none | adds its moves may spawn |
| `intro` | `{ line, title }` | from lines | 6.13 |
| `rebirth` | boolean | false | legendary only |
| `arena` | `prefers`: `open`, `tight`, `home` | `open` | adjusts move weights by surroundings |

**Removed fields** (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13; a file that still has one loads, and each
gets a NOTE in the v1 message format, so an old draft or an operator override says why it does nothing):

| Field | NOTE |
|---|---|
| `damage_vs_players` | "read and ignored: a hero deals the same damage to players and NPCs" |
| `defeat` (`{ yield }`) | "read and ignored: heroes do not yield; set `personality.when_losing` to choose between escaping and a last stand" |
| `attack_tokens`, `tokens` (never in a shipped file; the limit was the config key `boss.attackTokens`) | "read and ignored: any number of heroes may press one target" |
| `health_per_player`, `players` in `health_scale` (first-draft notes) | "read and ignored: boss health does not change with the number of attackers" |

How a losing fight ends is not a `boss` field: it belongs to the hero's nature, `personality.when_losing` and
`stands_for` (12.11, 6.13).

### 12.11 `personality`

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `temperament` | one of 30 (appendix A) | defaults for the traits, fight AI, tone, face, special rule |
| `traits` | map trait to 0-100 | each at most ±20 from the temperament default (ADJUSTED otherwise) |
| `when_losing` | `escape`, `last_stand` | optional; default from the traits and the temperament (6.13: last stand when aggression + honour - caution is 65 or more, appendix A). What the hero does when a fight goes badly: break off at its escape threshold, or fight until it dies. Synonyms (ADJUSTED): `flee`, `run`, `retreat` → `escape`; `stand`, `fight_to_death`, `never_flees` → `last_stand`; `yield`, `kneel`, `surrender` are refused (PART LEFT OUT: "heroes do not yield") and the default applies |
| `stands_for` | list of 0-2 of `home`, `leader` | optional; default from loyalty (`home` at 70 or more, `leader` at 85 or more). Where an `escape` hero fights to the death anyway (6.13); `[]`: nowhere. Ignored with a NOTE on a `last_stand` hero, which stands everywhere |
| `likes`, `dislikes` | 0-6 item ids each | gifts and recruitment (milestone 3) |
| `quirks` | 0-3 short texts | flavour for the hero sheet ("polishes her mallet when nervous") |

### 12.12 `leader`

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `eligible` | boolean (true) | in the leader draft |
| `title` | text 0-40 | "Lord of the Still Crane" |
| `faction_names` | 2-4 texts 3-32 | the first unused one is taken |
| `short_name` | text 1-14 | |
| `motto` | text 0-80 | |
| `colors` | `{ primary, secondary, accent }` | faction colours (usually from the hero's palette) |
| `banner` | `{ base, patterns: [ { pattern, color } ] }` | vanilla dyes and pattern ids, 2-4 layers; `mojang` refused |
| `base_style` | one of 16 families (appendix B) | |
| `culture` | `east`, `west`, `mixed` | recruits' names |
| `economy_focus` | 1-2 of `military`, `mining`, `farming`, `food`, `trade`, `craft`, `lore`, `building`, `scouting`, `defence`, `growth`, `people` | need order (5.5) |
| `seat` | optional noun | overrides the style's seat noun |

### 12.13 `talents` and duty

`talents`: 0-3 of `{ id, realm, affix, roles }` (section 7.2; `affix` and `roles` only for `aura`). The duty
(`behaviour.duty`) is the job it works when it is not leading.

### 12.14 `voice`

`{ blip, pitch, rate, hurt_pitch }`: `blip` from a fixed list of note-block instruments (`harp`, `bell`, `chime`,
`flute`, `xylophone`, `bit`, `hat`, `bass`, `guitar`, `pling`), played softly per syllable while a line shows; `pitch`
0.5-2.0 (default 1.25 female, 0.9 male, shifted by temperament); `rate` 2-4 ticks per syllable; `hurt_pitch` for the
vanilla player hurt and death sounds the hero uses.

Generated voices (S1, `SOUND.md` section 6): a hero with a sound set has its own blips, `factionclash:hero.voice.<id>`
(syllable variants made by `tools/sfxgen` from `herosrc/sfx/<id>.sfx.json`, played one per `rate` ticks with the
set's melody and jitter, levelled so a spoken line measures about -21, between a villager and a wandering trader);
`blip` and `pitch` remain the fallback for a hero without one, and `rate` drives both.

### 12.15 `lines`

v1 §23 events and rules, plus:

| Event | When | Placeholders |
|---|---|---|
| `founding` | it founds its faction at campaign start | `{name}`, `{faction}` |
| `intro` | boss intro | `{name}`, `{enemy}` (`{player}` is read as `{enemy}`, 6.15) |
| `phase_2`, `phase_3` | phase transitions | `{name}` |
| `enrage`, `stagger`, `counter` | those moments (rate-limited) | `{name}`, `{enemy}` |
| `escape` | it escapes: under its threshold in a losing fight, a lost raid, a lost base (6.13, `MOBILITY_AND_WORK.md` 7.7) | `{name}`, `{enemy}` |
| `last_stand` | a hero that will not or cannot leave drops under the health where it would have fled (once per fight, 6.13) | `{name}`, `{enemy}` |
| `raid_announce`, `base_lost`, `hero_fallen` | leader announcements | `{name}`, `{faction}`, `{target_faction}` |
| `work`, `night`, `small_talk` | idle moments at home | `{name}`, `{player}` |
| `pact_offer`, `pact_accepted`, `pact_refused`, `peace_offer`, `declare_war`, `betrayal`, `betrayed`, `stranger_warning`, `welcome`, `gift_liked`, `gift_disliked` | milestone 2 and 3 events (read now, used later) | as they need, plus `{item}` |

v1's `unlock` event never fires for a format-2 hero (nothing unlocks: section 8); format-1 files keep it. A hero's
death line is v1's `fallen` (v1 §23). The first draft's `yield` and `spared` events are gone: lines under them are
left out with a NOTE ("heroes do not yield; use `escape`, `last_stand` or `fallen`").

Each temperament has a fallback bank (`herosrc/temperaments/<t>.json`, 2-4 lines per event), so a hero needs only its
own voice for the events that matter: the authoring target is 40-60 lines per hero.

### 12.16 `recruit` and `relationships` (used from milestone 3)

`recruit`: `{ condition, value }` with `duel`, `payment`, `reputation`, `gift`, `visits`, `cause`, `rescue` (default by
temperament). `relationships`: 0-4 of `{ hero, kind }` with `friend`, `rival`, `sibling`, `mentor`, `student`,
`sworn_enemy`, `admirer`; checked across files (a relationship to an unknown hero is a NOTE).

### 12.17 Limits

v1 limits, plus: 12 moves, 3 states, 3 phases, 8 reactions, 4 weak points, 24 parts, 3 talents, 4 relationships, 12
lines per event; source file at most 256 KB, server file at most 128 KB.

### 12.18 Example (abridged)

The slice samurai (SLICE_PLAN.md section 3.5), as a source file; `// ...` marks parts left out here.

```jsonc
{
  "format": 2,
  "id": "kagenori_tsurumine",
  "name": "Kagenori Tsurumine",
  "title": "the Still Crane",
  "gender": "male",
  "archetype": "samurai",
  "rarity": "epic",
  "element": "shock",
  "resistances": { "shock": 20, "blight": -15 },
  "gear": [ { "item": "iron_sword", "name": "Crane's Edge", "enchantments": { "sweeping_edge": 2 } },
            "chainmail_chestplate", "iron_boots" ],
  "behaviour": { "combat_role": "striker", "duel_style": "counter", "duty": "guard", "focus": "strongest" },
  "basic_attack": { "style": "melee", "combo": 3,
                    "every_nth_hit": { "n": 3, "effects": [ { "type": "mark", "mark": "storm", "stacks": 1 } ] } },
  "moves": [
    { "id": "three_cut", "name": "Three Cuts", "form": "combo", "class": "light", "band": ["close"], "weight": 6,
      "strikes": [ { "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 55 } } ] },
                   { "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 55 } } ] },
                   { "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 80 } },
                                  { "type": "knockback", "strength": 1 } ] } ],
      "cooldown": 6 },
    { "id": "iai_stance", "name": "Still Water", "form": "counter", "class": "heavy", "band": ["close"],
      "stance": 1.5, "absorb": 80,
      "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 140 } }, { "type": "stun", "seconds": 1 } ],
      "prefer_when": { "condition": "target_is", "kind": "person" }, "cooldown": 10,
      "look": { "particle": "electric_spark", "sound": "item.shield.block" } },
    { "id": "thunderclap_draw", "name": "Thunderclap Draw", "form": "line", "class": "heavy", "band": ["mid"],
      "range": 8, "width": 1,
      "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 150 } } ],
      "followups": [ { "move": "crane_step", "chance": 30 } ], "cooldown": 11,
      "line": "Thunder answers the blade." },
    { "id": "crane_step", "name": "Crane Step", "form": "dash", "class": "heavy", "band": ["mid"], "range": 6,
      "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 80 } } ],
      "followups": [ { "move": "three_cut", "chance": 70 } ], "cooldown": 8 },
    { "id": "guard_splitter", "name": "Guard Splitter", "form": "melee", "class": "heavy", "breaks_guard": true,
      "band": ["close"], "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 110 } },
                                     { "type": "guard_break", "seconds": 4 } ], "cooldown": 12 },
    { "id": "chain_draw", "name": "Chained Lightning Draw", "form": "chain", "class": "heavy", "band": ["mid"],
      "range": 10, "radius": 4, "count": 3,
      "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": 7 }, { "type": "effect", "id": "slowness", "level": 1, "seconds": 3 } ],
      "cooldown": 14 },
    { "id": "falling_crane", "name": "Falling Crane", "form": "leap", "class": "heavy", "band": ["mid", "far"],
      "range": 8, "radius": 2,
      "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": { "attack_percent": 100 } }, { "type": "knockback", "strength": 2 } ],
      "followups": [ { "move": "three_cut", "chance": 100 } ], "cooldown": 12 }
  ],
  "ultimate": { "id": "heaven_split", "name": "Heaven-Splitting Draw", "form": "line", "class": "ultimate",
                "range": 12, "width": 2,
                "effects": [ { "type": "damage", "amount": 22 } ],
                "look": { "lightning": true, "sound": "entity.lightning_bolt.thunder" },
                "line": "Close your eyes. It is already over." },
  "passives": [
    { "name": "Still Mind", "trigger": "on_counter", "form": "self",
      "effects": [ { "type": "energy", "amount": 15, "to": "self" } ], "cooldown": 6 }
  ],
  "states": [ { "name": "twin_sky", "stats": [ { "stat": "attack_speed", "percent": 20 },
                                               { "stat": "damage_dealt", "percent": 10 } ],
                "show": ["p3_weapon_l"], "face": "determined", "line": "Two blades. No more restraint." } ],
  "boss": {
    "poise": 100, "weak_points": ["recovery", "back"],
    "phases": [
      { "at": 100, "moves": ["three_cut", "iai_stance", "thunderclap_draw", "crane_step"] },
      { "at": 60, "moves": ["three_cut", "iai_stance", "thunderclap_draw", "crane_step", "guard_splitter", "chain_draw"],
        "stats": [ { "stat": "armor", "percent": 20 } ],
        "transition": { "seconds": 1.2, "line": "Then I will fight you properly.", "face": "determined" } },
      { "at": 25, "moves": ["three_cut", "thunderclap_draw", "guard_splitter", "chain_draw", "falling_crane"],
        "states": ["twin_sky"], "transition": { "seconds": 1.4, "line": "Forgive me, teacher." } }
    ],
    "reactions": [ { "when": "target_blocking", "move": "guard_splitter", "chance": 60, "cooldown": 10 },
                   { "when": "target_drawing_bow", "move": "crane_step", "chance": 70, "cooldown": 8 } ],
    "enrage": { "after": 180 },
    "intro": { "line": "Tsurumine Kagenori. Draw when you are ready." }
  },
  "personality": {
    "temperament": "stoic",
    "traits": { "aggression": 50, "honour": 90, "ambition": 45, "caution": 65, "greed": 25,
                "sociability": 30, "loyalty": 85, "volatility": 10 },
    // "when_losing" is not written: 50 + 90 - 65 = 75, so he makes a last stand (6.13)
    "likes": ["bamboo", "cherry_sapling"], "dislikes": ["rotten_flesh"],
    "quirks": ["re-ties his hair before every duel"]
  },
  "leader": {
    "title": "Lord of the Still Crane",
    "faction_names": ["House of the Still Crane", "Still Crane Watch"],
    "short_name": "Still Crane",
    "motto": "The blade waits. So do we.",
    "colors": { "primary": "#2E3A6B", "secondary": "#E8E4D8", "accent": "#9AA6B2" },
    "banner": { "base": "blue", "patterns": [ { "pattern": "circle", "color": "white" },
                                              { "pattern": "border", "color": "light_gray" } ] },
    "base_style": "deepslate_keep", "culture": "east", "economy_focus": ["military", "mining"]
  },
  "talents": [ { "id": "drill" }, { "id": "mentor", "realm": true } ],
  "voice": { "blip": "bass", "pitch": 0.85, "rate": 3 },
  "lines": {
    "founding": ["From today this land is the Still Crane's. Keep your blades clean."],
    "greeting": ["{player}. Walk with purpose."],
    "kill": ["Rest."],
    "last_stand": ["I do not turn my back. Come."],
    "fallen": ["...Well struck."]
    // ... about 45 more lines
  },
  "art": {
    "base": "male", "form": "adult", "build": "standard", "scale": 102,
    "palette": { "skin": "#F1D3BC", "hair": "#1E1F2A", "eyes": "#6C7BD0", "primary": "#2E3A6B",
                 "secondary": "#E8E4D8", "accent": "#9AA6B2", "trim": "#B8963E", "metal": "#C9CED6" },
    "parts": [ { "part": "hair/high_ponytail_long", "color": "hair", "params": { "length": 11 } },
               { "part": "outfit_top/haori_crest", "color": "primary", "trim": "secondary", "pattern": "crane_crest" },
               { "part": "outfit_bottom/hakama", "color": "secondary" },
               { "part": "armour/shoulder_plate_r", "color": "metal" },
               { "part": "headwear/kabuto_plain", "color": "primary", "phase": 2 }
               // ... sash, sandals, wrist guards
             ],
    "face": { "eyes": "sharp", "pupil": "round", "iris": "#6C7BD0", "lashes": 0, "brows": "straight_thick",
              "mouth": "small", "overrides": { "happy": { "eyes": 8, "mouth": 1 } } },
    "weapon": { "prop": "weapons/katana_plain", "hand": "right", "sheathed": "hip" },
    "phases": [ { "phase": 3, "show": ["p3_weapon_l"], "eyes_glow": true } ],
    "glow": ["eyes_phase3", "blade_edge_phase3"]
  },
  "animations": {
    "style": "katana", "blink": "calm", "idle_quirks": ["hilt_rest", "eyes_closed_breath"],
    "signatures": {
      "move.thunderclap_draw": { "length": 2.2, "keys": [
        { "t": 0.0,  "pose": "katana.stance" },
        { "t": 0.25, "pose": "katana.iai_ready", "ease": "easeOutCubic", "face": "focused",
          "marker": "vfx:glint_hilt", "sound": "item.shield.block" },
        { "t": 1.2,  "pose": "katana.iai_ready_hold", "ease": "linear" },
        { "t": 1.3,  "pose": "katana.iai_cut_end", "ease": "easeOutExpo", "marker": "hit;vfx:slash_line" },
        { "t": 1.6,  "pose": "katana.iai_cut_end", "ease": "linear" },
        { "t": 2.2,  "pose": "katana.sheathe_end", "ease": "easeInOutSine" } ] }
      // ... one recipe per move, the ultimate, phase.2, phase.3, intro
    }
  }
}
```

The generator turns the `thunderclap_draw` recipe into a 2.2 s animation whose `hit` marker is at 1.3 s, and writes
`"frames": { "windup": 1.3, "active": 0.05, "recovery": 0.85 }` into the server file; the validator sees class
`heavy` (minimum 0.8 / 0.8) and accepts it.

---

## 13. Art pipeline

### 13.1 Principles

1. **Silhouette first, then colour blocks, then detail.** At Minecraft scale the head carries identity: ears, horns,
   hats, hair shape, halos, tails and wings. Every hero has one distinct head-silhouette element (ROSTER_PLAN.md
   "Look" column).
2. **Two or three main colours and one accent**, readable in greyscale (checked: luminance contrast between the
   largest two colour regions at least 15%).
3. **Shape language by temperament**: round shapes read friendly, square sturdy, spikes dangerous; the archetype and
   temperament templates bias part choice (a sweet-natured hero gets rounded hair and soft trims, a tyrant spikes).
4. **Anime look within pixel art**: hue-shifted shading, one-texel darker outlines on clothing and hair edges, a
   bright "ring" highlight band on hair, large expressive eyes at double resolution.
5. **Deterministic and editable**: the same inputs always give byte-identical outputs; every output opens in
   Blockbench; nothing is drawn by hand inside the pipeline, so 200 heroes stay consistent.

### 13.2 Sources (`herosrc/`, repository only)

```
herosrc/
  rig/rig_v3.json                bones, pivots (expressions over the base dims), required bones, joint limits, UV
                                 template regions, face layout, render scale, budgets
  bases/<form>_<f|m>.json        base dims per form and family, with slim and large width overrides (section 4.3);
                                 bases/_template.json holds the body cubes shared by all six
  style/<id>.hero.json           style fixtures: original characters on every base, built for the previews and tests
                                 only (never written to the mod's resources)
  parts/<category>/<part>.json   the parts library (13.3)
  palettes/presets.json          named palettes (calico, tuxedo, vermilion-white, steel-frost ...)
  faces/eyes/*.json, faces/mouths/*.json, faces/brows/*.json   face templates (13.5)
  patterns/*.json                procedural patterns (stripes, hemp-leaf, waves, petals, scales, lace, crests ...)
  anim/poses/*.json              named poses (13.6)
  anim/core/*.json, anim/styles/<style>.json   library animation recipes
  anim/secondary.json            spring groups of the follow-through bake (G3)
  archetypes/<archetype>.json    default parts, palette slots, base style, emblem, naming motifs, kit patterns
  temperaments/<t>.json          trait defaults, fight-AI defaults, blink profile, mood overrides, fallback lines
  heroes/<id>.hero.json          one per hero (section 12)
  clearance/<batch>.md           name and originality clearance records (ROSTER_PLAN.md section 8)
```

**UV template** (fixed regions, same for every hero; everything else is packed):

| Region (u, v, w, h) | Contents |
|---|---|
| 0, 0, 64, 128 | base body (packed: head sides and top at 1x, torso, arms, legs; rig v3's longer bodies) |
| 64, 0, 80, 30 | eye frames (4 x 3 of 20x10) |
| 144, 0, 20, 20 | head front at 2x (20x20 texels for the 10x10 face) |
| 64, 30, 64, 3 | mouth frames (8 of 8x3) |
| 64, 33, 64, 2 | cheek frames (4 of 16x2) |
| rest of 256x128 (or 256x256) | packed parts and weapon (first fit on an occupancy grid, largest first, 1-texel gutters) |

**Integer texels.** Many cube sizes are fractional (a 2.5 px arm, a 6.5 px hip). Box UV would put such faces on
half-texel boundaries, which nearest-neighbour sampling turns into uneven pixel widths and bleeding at seams. So every
face gets a **per-face UV rectangle of whole texels**: `round(face size x density)`, at least 1 (density 1, or 2 for the
head front and face planes). A 2.5 px face drawn with 3 texels stretches them by 17%, which is invisible in game and
keeps every pixel crisp. The packer works in whole texels only, and `herogen check` rejects any fractional UV. A part
or cube may set `density` 2 (two texels per pixel); rig v2's hair and ears did, rig v3's do not (the strands are cubes,
so 1 texel per px keeps them flat-painted like the references and cuts the hair's texels by four).

### 13.3 The parts library

A part is a small JSON file: cubes attached to rig bones (or new bones it declares), parametric sizes, colour slots,
paint instructions, and tags. Head parts are authored **head-local** (`"offset": [0, "head_y", 0]`: y 0 is the chin,
the face is at z -5 and the head spans x and z -5..5, y 0..10 on every form) and at double density. Example (abridged
from `hair/high_ponytail_long`):

```jsonc
{
  "id": "hair/high_ponytail_long",
  "category": "hair",
  "params": { "length": { "min": 6, "max": 16, "default": 12 }, "locks": { "min": 2, "max": 10, "default": 6 } },
  "occupies": ["hair_top", "hair_back"], "conflicts": ["hair/twin_tails*"],
  "offset": [0, "head_y", 0], "density": 2,
  "bones": [ { "name": "tail_hair_1", "parent": "head", "pivot": [0, 9.25, 5.75] },
             { "name": "tail_hair_2", "parent": "tail_hair_1", "pivot": [0, 3.25, 6.5] } ],
  "cubes": [
    { "bone": "hair_top", "origin": [-5.5, 4, -5.5], "size": [11, 6.75, 11], "faces": ["up", "south", "east", "west"] },
    { "bone": "hair_top", "origin": [-5, 10.75, -5], "size": [10, 0.75, 10] },
    { "bone": "bangs", "origin": [-5.5, 8.5, -6], "size": [11, 2.75, 1] },
    { "bone": "bangs", "origin": [-5.25, 6.5, -6.5], "size": [2.5, 4.5, 0.75], "taper": 0.3 },
    { "bone": "hair_r", "origin": [-6.6, "max(-locks, 3 - head_y)", -5.4],
      "size": [1.75, "10 - max(-locks, 3 - head_y)", 1.75], "taper": 0.5, "cutout": "tip_jagged_1", "mirror": true },
    { "bone": "tail_hair_1", "origin": [-1.5, 8.25, 5.5], "size": [3, 2, 2], "slot": "trim", "material": "cloth" },
    { "bone": "tail_hair_2", "origin": [-1.75, "max(3.25 - length * 0.6, 3.5 - head_y)", 6.25],
      "size": [3.5, "3.5 - max(3.25 - length * 0.6, 3.5 - head_y)", 2.75], "taper": 0.75 }
  ],
  "secondary": { "chain": ["tail_hair_1", "tail_hair_2", "fine_tail_hair_tip"], "stiffness": 0.35, "damping": 0.6 }
}
```

- **Parameters** are numbers or small expressions over the part's params (`"4 + length * 0.45"`), evaluated by the
  generator with a whitelist parser (no `eval`).
- **Tags**: `occupies` (slots such as `head_top`, `hair_back`, `ears`, `back`, `waist`, `hand_r`) and `conflicts`
  stop impossible combinations (a full helmet with cat ears needs the `ears_out` helmet variant); `fine_` cube names
  give level of detail; `phase` turns a part into a `p2_`/`p3_` part; `mirror: true` builds the left side from the
  right.
- **Cutouts** are alpha masks applied when painting (jagged bangs, fur tips, lace, feather edges, torn capes); GeckoLib
  renders `entityCutoutNoCull`, so cut-outs show from both sides.
- **Secondary** declares a chain for the follow-through bake (13.6).
- **Rig v2 additions** (all in `parts.py`'s docstring): part `offset` and `density`; cube `density`, `shade`
  (`vertical`: lit top and darker tip, the default for hair; `flat`; `mass`: no vertical shading or hem outline, top
  lit like the sides, for the stacked slabs of one solid such as an ear), `skip_if_empty` (an optional piece a param can
  switch off, such as an ahoge); paint op `vee` `[y_tip, slope]` (a V neckline); cutouts `tri_down`, `peak` (an animal
  ear: full width at the base, a point on top) and `round_top`. A cutout without a taper is cut through the cube's broad
  faces and the narrow faces and caps follow it, so an ear has no full-height strip on its sides. A `jagged_N` cut never
  cuts the outermost column on either side, so a jagged slab's narrow faces stay whole and its bottom face cannot show
  through them as a striped "barcode".
- **The hair kit** (style guide in `notes/artgen.md`; art direction pass 2: the crown is **parted**, 5 strands on each
  side of a centre line lying over the dome, replacing the radial whorl that read as a pinwheel; the crown is 0.5 px
  lower; every style paints the back of the head as hair so the nape is never bald): every hair style is built from a
  cap open at the front, a stepped dome, a fringe block over the forehead, 3-5 strand cubes over it whose tips stop at
  the lash line, side cover, back cover and the style's own pieces (long side locks, layered back strands, ponytails and twin tails on chains, an
  ahoge). Touching hair cubes of one colour are painted as one mass (no outline between them).
- **Style pass 2 additions** (rig v3, `gen.py`, `notes/artgen.md`): **generators** next to plain cubes: `strands` (rows
  of rotated boxes along a line or an arc, each hanging from an anchor or standing on it, with `flare` outward, `fan`,
  `zigzag` and `lean` sideways, `layers` of depth, cycled `length`, `width`, `tone`, a deterministic `jitter`,
  `bones_by_angle` for skirt panels, `mirror`, `clear_face` clipping, `fine_` by default: hair, bell sleeves, skirts,
  frills, tassets, capes), `chains` (bones along a polyline with a box per segment and rolled extra boxes: big tails,
  an ahoge) and `groups` (cubes sharing one rotation: an ear's stacked slabs, a bow's loops); `when` switches any of
  them off; cube `outline`. `rot.py` holds GeckoLib's rotation convention in file space (a file rotation (rx, ry, rz)
  applies Rz(-rz) Ry(ry) Rx(-rx)) and turns any wanted orientation into file angles. Named part cubes are prefixed
  with the part id. The library (37 parts): hair `long_layered`, `twin_tails`, `messy_short`, `short_crop`, `bob`,
  `high_ponytail_long`, `low_ponytail` (68-89 strand cubes each); ears `cat` (seven stacked 1 px slabs), `round`; tails
  `cat_long`, `fluffy` (five-bone chains); tops `kimono_wide` (bell sleeves, cuff frills, crossed lapels),
  `jacket_open` (lapels, hood, flared hem), `short_jacket` (sailor collar, ribbon bow); bottoms `flared_skirt` (panels
  and a frill hem), `pleated_skirt`, `hakama`, `coat_tails`; accessories `sash_bow`, `sash_obi`, `scarf`, `belt`,
  `apron`; armour `breastplate` (tassets), `pauldrons` (cap and two lames), `gauntlets` (flared cuff); back
  `cape_long` (fur mantle), `cape_short`; legwear `socks_shoes`, `boots`, `tights_boots`, `thigh_socks`,
  `tabi_sandals`, `greaves`; headwear `halo_ring`, `ribbon_bow`; `weapons/sword_plain`.
- **Built for the rig v2 style set** (2026-11-12; rebuilt for rig v3 in style pass 2): hair `short_crop`, `messy_short`, `bob`, `long_layered`,
  `twin_tails`, `high_ponytail_long`; ears `cat`, `round`; tails `cat_long`, `fluffy`; outfit tops `kimono_wide` (wide
  hanging sleeves, crossed collar), `jacket_open` (hood, lapels, flared hem), `short_jacket`; bottoms `flared_skirt`
  (three stacked widening tiers), `hakama`, `coat_tails`, `pleated_skirt`; `accessories/sash_bow` (the faction sash as an
  obi with a big bow), `sash_obi`, `scarf`; armour `breastplate`, `pauldrons` (layered plates), `gauntlets`; legwear
  `socks_shoes`, `boots`, `greaves`, `tights_boots`; back `cape_long` (fur mantle), `cape_short`; `headwear/halo_ring`.

| Category | Slice | Full library | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hair` | 12 | 40 | bob, hime cut, high and low ponytails, twin tails, side tail, braids, braid crown, buns, ringlets, wild mane, pompadour, short spiky, long straight, shaved sides, ahoge variants |
| `ears` | 4 | 12 | cat, tufted cat, fox, wolf, rabbit up, rabbit floppy, round, elf long, elf short, fin, curly lion-dog, bat |
| `horns` | 2 | 10 | single, twin short, twin curved, ram curl, antlers, swept dragon, demon straight, nubs, broken, crystal |
| `tails` | 3 | 10 | cat long, fox fluffy (instanced up to 9 with a fan layout), wolf bushy, rabbit puff, ringed, scaled, spade, fin, plume, shell |
| `wings` | 1 | 8 | crow, feathered white, bat, dragon, ember feather, mechanical, small paired, broken black |
| `headwear` | 8 | 34 | witch hats, court cap, small ascetic cap, straw hats, veiled hat, tricorne, crowns, tiara, hoods, fox and oni masks, long-nosed mask, helmets (kabuto, great helm, visor), halo, chef's hat, maid headdress, headbands, ribbons, hairpins, flower crown, goggles, beret, veil |
| `outfit_top` | 8 | 30 | short jacket, haori, kimono top, tabard, doublet, coat with tails, sleeveless top, hoodie, school coat, lab coat, vest, sailor top |
| `outfit_bottom` | 6 | 20 | pleated skirt, long skirt, hakama, trousers, shorts, armoured skirt, coat tails, wrap |
| `robes` | 3 | 12 | long kimono, furisode, priest robe, habit, hanfu, cassock, ceremonial gown |
| `armour` | 5 | 16 | lacquer plates, steel plate set, scale, shoulder plates, gauntlets, greaves, breastplate |
| `back` | 2 | 10 | capes (short, long, torn), drum ring, sword case, quiver, shell, backpack |
| `accessories` | 10 | 45 | scarves, chokers with bells, sashes, belts, prayer beads, bells, talisman strips, vial belts, glasses, monocle, eye patch, bandages, wrist guards, fur collars, epaulettes, earrings |
| `legwear` | 5 | 18 | thigh socks, tabi, sandals, clogs, boots, greaves, bare feet with wraps |
| `markings` | 3 | 12 | cheek stripes, scales, runes, freckles, scars, seams, tattoo lines |
| `weapons` | 10 | 70 | 3-5 props per style: katana, nodachi, claymore, kite and round shields, daggers, cleavers, rapier, glaive, spear, trident, club, mallet, maul, pan, axes, scythe, chain sickle, claws, gauntlets, bows, crossbows, staves, wands, talismans, lanterns, tomes, mirrors, flasks, fans, drums, lute, harp, horn, flute, microphone stand |

`herogen lint` builds every part on every base it allows and checks UV fit, budgets, conflicts and that mirrored parts
stay symmetric.

**As built (G6a and G6b, 2026-11-12; `notes/artgen.md`, "G6a and G6b")**: the slice column is complete, slice
heroes' needs first: 30 head parts (hair 12: new `hime_long`, `wild_mane`, `half_up_long`, `braid_crown`,
`messy_medium`, and `bob` gained `tufts` and `ahoge`; ears 4: new `fox`, `cat_tufted`; horns `single_curved`,
`twin_short`; tails 3: new `fox`, up to nine fanned copies; wings `feathered`; headwear 8: new `witch_hat`,
`fox_mask`, `half_mask`, `kabuto`, `visored_helm` with its visor on its own bone, `headband`) and 34 body parts (tops
8: new `wrap_top`, `kimono_top`, `haori`, `tabard`, `open_coat`; bottoms 6: new `shorts`, `trousers`; robes
`court_robe`, `long_coat`, `priest_robe`; armour 5: new `sode`, `bracers`; legwear 9: new `geta`, `foot_wraps`,
`tabi_boots`; back 3: new `standard`). Format additions: strand `tip` (the end in another slot), the entry's `left`
slots for mirrored copies, chain `copies`/`fan`, paint globs on the part's own cubes (`#bell*`), a `mask` bone that
may cover the mouth. Layers follow a convention (px out from the body: the sash is the outermost waist layer at 0.6,
leg shells stop 0.05 px short of the centre line, nested shells are 0.15 px apart) so two parts never share a
plane. The slice heroes' looks are the sample outfits of `herosrc/style/outfits.json`. `herogen lint` (`lint.py`):
every part alone on the six bases with a sword at the hip (the build, the asset checks, the ground, the coplanar
rule in the idle and combat stances), on the slim and large builds and at param extremes (geometry), static rules
(conflicts name parts, `occupies` declared, exact mirrors), then the sample outfits on the six bases; about 10
minutes. Sheets: `docs/heroes-v2/previews/parts/` (`herogen parts-previews [--check]`).

**As built (G6c, 2026-11-13; `notes/artgen.md`, "G6c")**: the rest of the slice column, 94 parts in all. Accessories
15 (10 new: `choker_bell`, `prayer_beads`, `foxfire_lanterns` on their own bones, `rope_belt`, `gourd` on a sprung
bone, `talisman_strips`, `talisman_ring` (Hinae's phase 2), `kunai_holster`, `vial_bandolier`, `crow` with a head
bone), markings 3 (`calico` patches over hair and tail, `arm_stripes` that glow in the phase look, `crest`: the hero
entry's emblem on the back, chest or sleeves) and weapons 12 (11 new: `claw_gauntlet`, `folding_fan`, `iron_club`,
`purification_wand`, `talisman_fan`, `katana` (long and short), `short_blade`, `kunai`, `longsword`, `kite_shield`,
`cauldron_staff`). A weapon part names its `hand` (right, left, pair, both with a second grip), `grip` (forward or
reverse), `sheath` (hip, obi, back, back up, flat on the back, across the small of the back, worn, none) and `style`;
a hero's weapon may add an `offhand` prop, drawn from a phase (its holstered copy is hidden by the phase), and a
`mount` (Ambrose's staff-broom: a `mount` bone under `root` with a copy of the staff at the seat height, for G10).
Every slice weapon is checked in each hand pose of its style on all six bases (`grips.py`: clear of the body and the
ground, the second hand on a two-handed prop, no coplanar faces with it drawn), in `herogen lint` and the tests;
the hand poses (`herosrc/anim/poses/<style>.json`) were solved so the props clear a chibi's head too. Sheets:
`details_accessories.png`, `weapons.png`, `weapons_poses.png`, `patterns_emblems.png`.

### 13.4 Palette and procedural painting (`paint.py`, `palette.py`)

- **Ramps**: each palette slot becomes a 5-tone ramp in OKLCH: tones -2..+2 change lightness by 0.08 per step; shadows
  shift hue toward blue-violet by up to 12 degrees and lose chroma, highlights shift toward yellow by up to 8 degrees
  (the hue-shifting of pixel-art shading). Skin ramps shift shadows toward red instead. **Hair ramps** (art direction
  pass 2, the `hair` and `hair_accent` slots) step 0.085 down and 0.095 up. Their shadows keep their chroma and lean
  orange-red for warm hair, or blue-violet for cool hair, so gold reads as gold-to-orange as in the references. Dark
  hair (L below 0.42) steps 0.12 up toward a cool blue sheen, so a black mass still shows its strands.
- **Materials** decide how a face of a cube is painted: `cloth` (soft vertical gradient, folds every 3-4 texels on long
  faces), `skin` (soft, blush region on the face plane only), `hair` (style pass 2: painted flat, 2-3 tones per face
  and no strand lines, the strands being cubes; art direction pass 2: the top 30% of a strand's side lit, the lowest
  40% one tone darker, the tip row the darkest; earlier: every strand cube lit at the top fifth,
  darker in its lower third, outlined at its tip; strand lines every 4 texels at 2x and short highlight streaks; no
  crown ring), `metal` (high contrast, a specular band, rim light), `leather` (mid contrast, stitch lines), `fur` (noise
  tufts, cut-out tips), `scale` (overlapping arcs), `wood`, `paper` (flat, ink marks), `lacquer` (glossy, sharp
  highlight), `bone`, `gem` and `glow` (bright, written to the glow mask).
- **Light baked by face direction**: top +1 tone, front and back 0, sides 0 and -1 (light from the hero's front-left),
  bottom -2; a one-tone darker band on the lowest third of tall faces; one-tone ambient occlusion where a child cube
  meets its parent (collars, cuffs, belts).
- **Outlines**: a one-texel band two tones darker on the silhouette-relevant edges of clothing and hair (bottom and side
  edges); never across skin interiors.
- **Patterns**: stripes, plaid, checks, dots, waves, hemp-leaf, petals, scales, fur tufts, lace, trim bands, and
  emblem stamps from the archetype (paw, flame, bell, crest, star, moon disc, leaf), each with 1-3 colour
  slots and a scale; applied per cube face with seams aligned across neighbouring faces.
  As built (G6c): `stripes`, `checks`, `plaid`, `dots`, `pleats`, `waves`, `scales`, `hemp`, `petals`, `patches`
  (seeded soft blobs: calico), `solid` and the `lace` cutout; **overlay** paint ops keep each texel's own slot and
  shading and add only a pattern or an emblem; **emblem stamps** (`emblems.py`: paw, flame, bell, crest, star, moon,
  leaf, lily, crane, plum, and small eye, slit and script marks, each drawn by hand at 5-11 texels) on a cube face or
  at a point of a paint op, the hero entry's `emblem` through `"@"`, flipped on mirrored copies and kept upright
  through a weapon's carry; `glow: "phase"` lights a region only in the phase texture.
- **Glow mask and phase texture**: `glow` regions write `<id>_glowmask.png`; any phase palette change writes
  `<id>_p2.png` (and `_p2_glowmask.png`).
- **Checks**: adjacent colour regions differ by delta-E 10 or more; the greyscale contrast rule of 13.1; no pure black
  or pure white outside eyes and highlights.

### 13.5 Faces (`face.py`)

- **Face system** (style pass 2, rig v3; full table in `notes/artgen.md`): eye **shape families** `round`, `wide`,
  `almond`, `sharp` (tsurime), `droopy` (tareme), `narrow`, `sleepy` (jitome), `heavy_lid`, `cat`, `small`, each a
  per-column opening so the shape carries the lash slope and the corners; **iris styles** `gradient`, `two_tone`,
  `ring`, `solid`, `glossy` (and `iris2` for heterochromia); **pupils** `round`, `dot`, `slit`, `none`, `star`,
  `ring`; **highlights** `one`, `two`, `sparkle`, `inner`, `none`; lash weight 0-3 with an outer **flick** up, down or
  none; **spacing** 2-8; **brows** `thin_arched`, `straight`, `straight_thick`, `worried`, `bold`, `angled`, `short`,
  `none`; **mouths** `small`, `soft`, `wide`, `cat`, `stern`, `fang`, `pout`, `smirk`; **blush** `soft`, `hatch`,
  `dots`; **marks** freckles, beauty mark, scar, face paint, stubble, bandage; **wear** glasses (round, square, half)
  and an eye patch. All of it is drawn into every frame it crosses, over one skin layer, so blinking and the 12 moods
  keep working; `face.distinct` counts the features two faces differ in (review gate 2 and the style fixtures' test).
  The closed eye is a dark line low across the eye (the references' dropped lash bar).
- **Eye templates** (rig v2 text, kept for history; rig v3 replaces the rows below with the layout of 4.6 and the
  shapes above) (parametric, on the 20x10 eye plane of rig v2): `round`, `almond`, `sharp`, `droopy`, `cat`,
  `sleepy`, `wide`, `narrow`, `heavy_lid`; parameters: width 5-7 texels, height 4-6 (plus the lash row), spacing 2, 4
  or 6, iris size 3-5 (at most width - 2), pupil type, one or two highlights, lash weight 0-3 (1 a dark line, 2 adds the
  outer corner, 3 an upward flick), lower lash on or off (on for female faces). Texel layout (the same for every hero,
  so frames line up): brows in rows 0-1 (rest brows on row 1), row 2 skin, the upper lash in row 3, the eye body in rows
  4-9; the eyes mirror about the plane's centre line and keep their body inside columns 1-18 (a lash flick may reach
  column 0 or 19), so a 7-wide eye at spacing 4 fills columns 1-7 and 12-18. The iris runs dark under the lash to light
  at the bottom, a 2x2 pupil sits in its middle, a 2x2 white highlight in its upper outer part (the white beside it is
  shadowed under the lash, so the highlight reads) and a small second highlight low on the inner side. Brow templates: `thin_arched`, `straight`,
  `straight_thick`, `worried`, `bold`, `none` (under bangs).
- **Eye frames** generated from the template by fixed rules:

| Frame | Rule |
|---|---|
| 0 open | the template |
| 1 half | upper lid line lowered to half the eye height; iris top hidden |
| 2 closed | one lash line on the lower third, curved down for gentle temperaments, straight for stern ones |
| 3 happy | an upward arc with lashes |
| 4 angry | brows angled down toward the centre by 1-2 texels; upper lid flattened inward; iris 1 texel smaller |
| 5 sad | brows angled up toward the centre; lids droop outward; a highlight tear point |
| 6 surprised | iris 1 texel smaller, more white, brows raised 1 texel |
| 7 pain | `>` `<` shapes |
| 8 focused | lids narrowed to 60%, brows slightly down, iris whole |
| 9 smug | half lid plus one raised brow |
| 10 glowing | iris in the glow colour, written to the glow mask |
| 11 sleeping | closed, relaxed brows |

- **Mouth templates**: `small`, `wide`, `soft`, `cat`, `stern`; frames: neutral line, smile curve, talk A (small
  oval), talk B (open with a tooth row), grit (teeth), frown, cat mouth, fang grin (fangs from `fang`). **Cheeks**:
  none, blush (two soft ovals), sweat drop, tears.
- **Overrides** per hero (`art.face.overrides`, written to `visual.json`) map a mood to other frames.
- **Checks**: left and right eyes mirror unless a marking or eye patch says otherwise; every open-type frame has a
  highlight; closed frames stay inside the eye's bounds; frames differ from each other.

### 13.6 Animations (`anim/`)

- **As built (G3, 2026-11-12; details in `notes/artgen.md`, "G3")**: modules `anim/clip.py` (GeckoLib's keyframe
  semantics and easings), `skeleton.py` (forward kinematics), `poses.py`, `primitives.py`, `recipes.py`,
  `secondary.py`, `writer.py` (animation and timing files), `quality.py`, `library.py`, `timing.py`, `preview.py`;
  sources `herosrc/anim/{poses,core,styles}/*.json` and `herosrc/anim/secondary.json`. Generated: `_core` (`move.idle`
  24 s, `move.walk` 1.0 s, `move.run` 0.6 s) and `_style_katana` (`combat.idle`, `attack.1` with its cut at clip time
  0.40 s; its `hit` is written at 0.50 s, tick 10 from the action's start, after the action controller's 2-tick blend:
  4.8). One file serves every form: bone names are shared, and pixel offsets that depend on leg length (the root
  height of a gait, the leg tilt under a weight shift, a knee bend) are solved over the three forms as the compromise
  with the smallest worst error (within 0.5 px on all six bases). The sign conventions (a forward limb swing is
  negative rx, elbow flexion negative rx, knee flexion positive rx, +ry turns right, +rz raises `arm_r` sideways) are
  in `anim/poses.py`; the rig's joint limits carry the flex sign.
- **Pose library** (`herosrc/anim/poses/*.json`): named poses as bone rotations (degrees) and an optional root offset;
  poses extend others (`katana.windup` extends `katana.stance`), `mirror` builds the other side, `knees` bends the knees
  with planted feet, and the fixes `level_feet` and `ground` put the soles flat on the ground. Shared poses are
  authored once and checked against joint limits.
- **Primitives** (Python functions with parameters): `walk_cycle`, `run_cycle`, `idle_breathe`, `swing`, `thrust`,
  `slam`, `spin`, `leap`, `land`, `cast`, `draw_bow`, `fire_crossbow`, `throw`, `guard`, `dodge`, `hurt`, `stagger`,
  `kneel`, `death`, `greet_bow`, `wave`, `cheer`, `taunt`, `play_instrument`, `work_loop`. G3 built `walk_cycle`,
  `run_cycle`, `breathe`, `weight_shift`, `look` and `ear_twitch`; G4 built `crouch_cycle`, `carry_cycle`,
  `oscillate` (waves, nods, shakes, pumps, stirring, a dizzy circle) and `flinch` (a damped impulse), and authored the
  keyed motions (dodge, hurt, stagger, kneel, death, greet_bow, wave, cheer, the work strokes) as pose keys with those
  layers on top; the attack primitives come with G5 (G3's swing is pose keys).
- **Recipe**: `{ length, loop, keys: [ { t, pose | primitive + params, ease, root, face, marker, sound, particle,
  overshoot, settle } ] }` (example in 12.18). `ease` uses GeckoLib's easing names; `overshoot` adds a key past the pose
  with `easeOutBack`; `settle` adds a small return. `face` writes `face_ctrl` keys with `step` easing. As built (G3):
  primitives are `layers` (`[{primitive, params, add}]`, added to the pose keys or replacing the channels they key)
  rather than a field of a key; `bones` limits what a recipe keys (an attack keys the upper body, so the legs keep
  walking under it); `overshoot` k is an explicit key k x the last change past the pose and a return 0.1 s later;
  face changes are pre/post jumps, never `step` (4.6); `class`, `settle_to`, `kind`, `planted`, `ground`.
- **Secondary motion bake**: chains declared by parts (hair, ponytails, tails, ears, capes, skirts, sleeves) follow
  their parents through a damped spring simulated at 20 Hz; the result is keyed every 2 ticks and simplified (keys
  within 0.5 degrees dropped). At runtime, the `secondary` controller and the procedural lag add movement-driven sway.
  As built (G3): the shared libraries bake the rig's chain bones (`tail_1..5`, `tail_hair_1..3`, `ear_*`,
  `hair_back/l/r`, `sleeve_*`, `skirt_f/b/r/l`, `cape_1/2`) with the group settings of `herosrc/anim/secondary.json`;
  the spring answers the parent's motion only (a bone's own authored turn, an ear twitch, is kept sharp) and `hang`
  pulls sleeves, capes, ponytails, skirts and hair toward their bind direction like gravity; the chains a hero's parts
  declare and their stiffness and damping go to `visual.json` `secondary` for the procedural lag (4.4). G3 review: an
  action is baked over its settle clip as authored (that clip's own follow-through is not counted twice), bakes only
  the chain bones below the bones it keys (a swing leaves the skirt panels and the tail to the walk under it), and a
  bell sleeve's `hang` fades out (`hang_fade`) while the forearm is raised well above horizontal, with its lag behind
  the hang capped (`lag`), so its open end does not face the viewer as a disc.
- **Markers**: `hit`, `vfx:<id>`, `sfx:<sound>` become GeckoLib custom-instruction keyframes in `timeline`, sound
  keyframes in `sound_effects`, particle keyframes in `particle_effects`. Several instructions at one time are joined
  with `;`.
- **Output**: GeckoLib animation JSON (`format_version` 1.8.0) with per-bone `rotation`, `position` and `scale`
  channels, keyframes written the way Blockbench's GeckoLib export writes them (vector plus easing); the exact keys are
  checked against GeckoLib's keyframe parser when the writer is built. Checked (G3): GeckoLib reads a channel's keys in
  file order, so keyframe maps are written in ascending time order (not sorted as strings); a test ports its parser
  and reads every written channel back.
- **Quality rules** (`herogen check`): the anticipation before every `hit` meets the class minimum; joint limits
  (elbow and knee flex one way, 0-150 degrees; head yaw ±80, pitch ±60; waist twist ±45); feet stay planted (forward
  kinematics, drift at most 0.5 px) unless a key marks a step, leap or dodge; loops close (first key = last key); no
  animation shorter than its last marker; no root motion (movement is physical on the server); every one-shot action
  animation ends on the first key of its style's `combat.idle` (work and emotes: `move.idle`) for each bone it keys,
  because GeckoLib snaps rather than blends when the action controller stops (4.8); `face_ctrl` keys are whole numbers.
  As built (G3), on all six bases: planted clips keep every sole corner within 0.5 px, grounded clips keep the lowest
  sole within 0.6 px of the ground, gaits never sink a sole more than 0.6 px; root x and z within 1.5 px; neck yaw ±60
  and pitch ±40 added; face_ctrl changes are jumps; the timing file agrees with the markers. G3 review: an action
  that starts on its settle clip's pose starts there on its baked chain bones too; markers come before the last tick;
  timing files state the controller blend.

**Core library** (`_core.animation.json`, about 45 animations): `move.idle`, `move.walk`, `move.run`, `move.swim`,
`move.fall`, `move.climb`, `move.crouch`, `rest.sit`, `rest.sleep`, `carry.loop`, the 16 `work.*` of section 7.3, 10
`emote.*` (`greet`, `bow`, `wave`, `laugh`, `angry`, `sad`, `think`, `cheer`, `talk`, `shrug`), `hurt.heavy`,
`stagger`, `stun` (`.loop`), `interrupt`, `dodge`, `death`, `defeated` (`.loop`), `intro`, `victory`, `personal.*`
generics.

As built (G4, 2026-11-12; `notes/artgen.md`, "G4"): 55 animations, all of the list plus `work.fish_wait.loop` and the
appendix D codes no other file serves (`eat`, `spared`, `enrage`, `founding`); every one passes `herogen check` on all
six bases. Generator additions: **form-scaled keys** (a root height written as `"v + k * v.fc_dhip"`, exact on every
form once `HeroModel` sets the variable, 4.4); the **`floor` fix** (the lowest point of the base body, a knee, a
seat, a back, on the ground on every form); the root **following the floor** between two grounded keys; kneels solved
with a two-link IK so the front foot stays put and the back foot steps; the **slide rule** (`steps`: a foot on the
ground never skates) and **body contact** (`contact: body`); `"@stance"` and `over` keys relative to the stance an
action plays over; **restyled reactions** (`restyle: true`: every style library with a `combat.idle` gets its own
copy of `hurt.heavy`, `stagger`, `stun` (+`.loop`), `interrupt`, `dodge`, `death`, `defeated` (+`.loop`), `intro`,
`victory`, `enrage`, ending on its own stance); skirt panels that follow the thighs of a sit or a kneel. Previews:
`previews/anim/core/` (movement, work with tool props, work at home, emotes, combat over move.idle and over the katana
stance; GIFs and contact sheets on a chibi and an adult; `herogen anim-previews --core --check`). G4 review
(`notes/artgen.md`, "G4 review"): the files load in GeckoLib 4.9.3's own parser with every key and event as herogen
reads them; the tool strokes were re-solved for the grip convention (4.7) so they read as the work (the axe flat
into a trunk at chest height, the hoe into the soil, the hammer face on the anvil, the broom's bristles on the floor,
the rod out over the water; `work.till` now keys `body`, still no leg) and tests pin them on the teen form; `death`
is 0.75 s (inside vanilla's death ticks with the blend) and its head no longer sinks; the settle rule has a failing
test.

**Style libraries** (16, about 14 animations each): `combat.idle`, `move.run` arm carry, `draw`, `sheathe`,
`attack.1-3` (a string that reads as that weapon), `attack.ranged` where it has one, `guard` (`.loop`, `.hit`),
`dodge`, `intro`, `victory`, `taunt`, and style versions of `work.chop` and `work.mine` where it matters.

| Style | Stance and string |
|---|---|
| `katana` | low ready; horizontal, diagonal, thrust; quick-draw ready and sheathe |
| `greatblade` | shoulder carry; heavy arcs with body turn; overhead |
| `sword_shield` | shield forward; cut, cut, bash; raised guard |
| `twin_blades` | crouched reverse grip; four quick slashes; spin |
| `rapier` | side-on; thrust, thrust, flick; riposte |
| `polearm` | two-handed mid guard; thrust, sweep, vault; throw |
| `hammer` | low two-handed carry; overhead, side swing, slam |
| `axe` | chop, chop, spin; throw |
| `scythe` | wide reaping sweeps; hook pull |
| `fists` | guard up; jab, jab, kick; grab and throw |
| `bow` | draw, release, kneeling aim, volley |
| `crossbow` | shoulder aim, fire, reload |
| `staff` | caster ready; cast forward, cast up; spin bonk |
| `focus` | one-hand cast, throw, channel |
| `fan` | dance steps; gust; fan cut |
| `instrument` | play loop; beat; chord blast |

As built for the slice (G5, 2026-11-13; `notes/artgen.md`, "G5"): `fists`, `focus`, `hammer`, `katana`,
`twin_blades`, `sword_shield` and `staff`, each with the whole list above (`guard.hit.loop` holds a guard after a
blow; the run that carries the weapon is named `combat.run`, the counterpart of `combat.idle`, so a hero with its
weapon sheathed keeps the core's `move.run`: A2's base controller should try `combat.run` first while the weapon is
drawn), `attack.ranged` for the three styles whose heroes throw (focus: a toss; twin_blades: the kunai; staff: a vial),
`work.mine` for the hammer (Benika mines two-handed) and `work.chop` for the katana (7.3), and the core's restyled
reactions over each stance; 24-25 clips per library, every one passing `herogen check` on all six bases.

| Style | Slice prop | Stance | String (hit at clip time) | Guard |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fists` | Natsuho's claws | guard up | lead jab 0.40, cross 0.40, claw rake 0.45 | forearms crossed |
| `focus` | Hinae's wand and talismans, Yugen's fan | casting ready | one-hand cast 0.40, off-hand flick 0.40, two-hand burst 0.45; toss 0.40 | a ward |
| `hammer` | Benika's club (two hands) | club on the shoulder | overhead 0.45, side swing 0.45, slam 0.55 (the knees give, the root form-scaled) | the club as a bar |
| `katana` | Kagenori's sword | low ready | diagonal 0.40, level 0.40, thrust 0.45; quick draw and chiburi | blade across |
| `twin_blades` | Tobimaru's reversed blade and kunai | crouched | backhand slash 0.40, kunai stab 0.40 + slash 0.60, whirl 0.45; kunai throw 0.40 | crossed before the face |
| `sword_shield` | Ilsabet's sword and kite shield | shield forward | diagonal cut 0.40, level cut 0.40, shield bash 0.45 | shield high |
| `staff` | Ambrose's cauldron staff | caster ready | forward cast 0.40, upward cast 0.45, the bonk 0.50; vial toss 0.40 | the staff as a bar |

Every string attack is class light (the server's frames come from its `hit`: wind-up = clip time + the 2-tick blend,
4.8), keys the upper body and hips only (the hammer's slam also bends the knees, feet planted), and settles on the
stance; `draw` plays over `move.idle` and settles on `combat.idle`, `sheathe` the other way, and their
`weapon:drawn` / `weapon:sheathed` markers are the ticks at which the server swaps the weapon flag (the timing files
list them). Marker sounds are vanilla events (SOUND.md 5) until style kits exist. G5 generator additions: a style
file's own in-between `poses`, `plays_over`, `arms_over` with the style file's `restyle_arms` (a restyled death,
kneel or stagger lowers the weapon arm instead of folding the guard into the head), `run_cycle` with `upper: 0` (a
run that carries its weapon), an error for a `settle` or `overshoot` key that lands after the next pose key, the
form-scaled `floor` fix on the deep twin-blades crouch, and `grips.animation_problems` (the props through every tick
of every clip on all six bases, `tests/test_styles.py`). Previews: `docs/heroes-v2/previews/anim/styles/` (the string
and the rest of the set as GIFs on a chibi and an adult holding the slice prop, a contact sheet per style;
`herogen anim-previews --styles [--check]`).

**Per hero**: `move.<id>` for every move, `ultimate` (and `.loop`, `.end` for channelled ones), `phase.2`, `phase.3`,
`intro`, `state.<name>`, 1-3 `personal.*`; typically 10-16 animations.

### 13.7 Tooling (`tools/herogen/`)

`python3 tools/herogen/herogen.py <command>` (Python 3.10+, Pillow, NumPy; the tool checks for them and says what to
install):

| Command | What |
|---|---|
| `new <n or id>` | scaffold `herosrc/heroes/<id>.hero.json` from a roster row plus the archetype and temperament templates |
| `build [ids \| --batch N \| --slice \| --all \| --changed]` | write the model, texture, glow and phase textures, animation file, `visual.json` and the server definition |
| `build --check` | rebuild into a temporary folder and fail if anything differs from the committed files |
| `check [ids \| --all]` | asset validation (14.2), then `./gradlew checkHeroes` for the kit rules (14.1) |
| `preview <id> [--anim NAME] [--action NAME] [--t 0.4 \| --frames 8] [--view 34\|front\|side\|right\|back\|34back\|top] [--yaw DEG] [--phase 2] [--mood angry] [--night] [--ties] [--scale 4] [--committed]` | render PNGs with the software renderer (13.8); `--ties` paints coplanar faces of different colour (they z-fight in game) magenta and lists their bones |
| `gif <ids...> [--anim NAME] [--action NAME] [--fps 20] [--view ...]` | an animated preview (heroes side by side) |
| `faces <id>` | every eye, mouth and cheek frame enlarged, plus each mood, and the rendered face strip |
| `sheet [ids] [--style] --out FILE` | contact sheets, 24 heroes each (`--batch`/`--slice` come with the roster) |
| `render-previews [--only ...] [--check]` | the owner's G2 set in `docs/heroes-v2/previews/render/`; `--check` renders it in a temporary folder and fails if a committed preview differs (stale) |
| `lint [parts]` | the parts library on every base (builds, asset checks, the ground, coplanar faces; builds and param extremes) and the sample outfits (G6a/b, `lint.py`) |
| `parts-previews [--only ...] [--check]` | the parts sheets in `docs/heroes-v2/previews/parts/` (G6a/b) |
| `stats` | budgets per hero and library usage |
| `import-anim <id> <file>` | adopt a Blockbench-polished animation file (sets `animations.source: manual`) |

Modules: `rig.py`, `parts.py`, `uvpack.py`, `palette.py`, `paint.py`, `face.py`, `geo.py`, `anim/poses.py`,
`anim/primitives.py`, `anim/recipes.py`, `anim/secondary.py`, `anim/writer.py`, `kit.py` (server definition and
frames), `visual.py`, `validate.py`, `render/raster.py`, `render/sheet.py`, `render/gif.py` (as built for G2 also
`render/animfile.py`, `render/face_sim.py`, `render/scene.py`, `render/showcase.py`), `scaffold.py`, `cli.py`;
tests in `tools/herogen/tests/` (`python3 -m unittest discover tools/herogen/tests`). Determinism: sorted keys, numbers
rounded to 3 decimals, randomness seeded from the hero id, PNGs written without metadata. As built for G3 the
animation side also has `anim/clip.py`, `anim/skeleton.py`, `anim/quality.py`, `anim/library.py`, `anim/timing.py`
(the move frames `kit.py` will write) and `anim/preview.py`, and the commands `anim` (list the libraries) and
`anim-previews` (filmstrips and GIFs); keyframe maps keep their time order (`util.Ordered`). G4 added
`anim-previews --core` and G5 `anim-previews --styles` (both with `--check`: render in a temporary folder and fail on
a stale committed preview).

Gradle: `./gradlew checkHeroes` (Java kit validator over `data/factionclash/heroes`, as v1's task) runs in `check`;
`./gradlew verifyHeroAssets` (runs `herogen build --check`, needs Python) is separate so a machine without Python can
still build. The Java `HeroAssetsTest` (14.2) runs in the normal unit tests without Python.

### 13.8 Preview renderer and screenshots

- **Software renderer** (`render/raster.py`, NumPy): reads the `.geo.json`, evaluates an animation at time t with
  GeckoLib's semantics (keyframe interpolation and easings, bone rotation order Z then Y then X, the pivot and
  rotation sign conversions GeckoLib applies when baking), builds cube quads with their UVs, rasterises them with a
  z-buffer and nearest-neighbour texture sampling, alpha cut-out at 0.1 like `entityCutoutNoCull`, and shades faces
  with vanilla's entity light directions so the result looks like the game. Output is pixel-crisp at 4x.
- **As built (G2, 2026-11-12; `notes/artgen.md`)**: the light is vanilla's world-space entity light (front and back
  0.74, sides 0.50, top 1.0, bottom 0.4 for a hero facing south or north; it does not follow the camera), normals go
  through GeckoLib's flat-cube fix, the glow layer is lit but ignores the light map, animations are read from the
  written files and played through an emulation of the base and action controllers (the action's 2-tick blend,
  events on the frame they fire), and the face comes from `visual.json` through a stand-in for `HeroFace` (moods,
  `face_ctrl`, seeded blinks). The camera is orthographic for previews and perspective for the fidelity check. Found
  by it: the zero-thickness face planes are lit apart from the head when the face points down or to one side
  (GeckoLib's flat-cube fix); recommended: A2 skips the fix for those bones. The G2 review added the coplanar-face
  check (`raster.coplanar_ties`, `preview --ties`, `previews/render/coplanar_faces.png`): the renderer settles a depth
  tie by draw order, the game z-fights, so two coplanar faces of different colour are drawn a second time in reverse
  order and every pixel that changes is reported (the sash layers of three style fixtures fight their coat or skirt;
  fixed in G6b, where it became a `check` rule); and `render-previews --check` (two committed GIFs had gone stale).
- **Fidelity check** (task G2, with A2): the test rig in three poses is rendered by the preview and screenshotted in
  game; the silhouettes must match within one pixel at 4x. This guards the sign and order conventions.
- **Contact sheet**: 24 heroes per sheet (6 x 4 cells); each cell has the 3/4 front view, a small back view, the phase
  look, the face strip (open, blink, happy, angry, focused, pain, talk), a palette swatch, and the name, archetype,
  role, element and weapon.
- **In game**: `/fc admin hero lineup [from] [count] [spacing]` places heroes with AI off in a row facing the caller;
  `/fc admin hero preview <id> <animation> [loop]` plays one animation; `/fc admin hero pose <id> mood <m> | phase <n>`
  sets the face or phase look. Screenshots come from the Xvfb `clientViewer` flow of HANDOFF section 4 (spectator
  camera placed with `tp`, `import -window root`).
- **Blockbench round trip**: geometry and animation files open with the GeckoLib plugin. A hand-polished animation is
  adopted with `import-anim`, after which the generator keeps that file and reads its frames from its markers; a
  hand-edited model or texture is a new part or a per-hero override part, never an edit of generated output.

---

## 14. Validation and tests

### 14.1 Kit validation (Java, `logic/hero/`, the v1 pipeline extended)

`HeroJson` -> `HeroReader` (catalogue-driven, format 1 and 2) -> sense rules -> `HeroBalance` -> across files. Format 2
adds these rules (same outcomes and message format as v1 §26):

- moves: unique ids; class minimums for wind-up and recovery (ADJUSTED); a move that stuns an enemy raised to at
  least `heavy` (ADJUSTED, 6.4); each move's `anim` exists in the generated
  move list; followups and reaction moves exist; followup strings at most 4 moves long, and a move may follow itself only
  when it is `repeatable` (the string limit then stops it); `per_shot` only on
  `barrage`; new forms' parameters in range; new effects' recipient classes; cooldown floors with the new weights
  (6.5);
- boss: phases from 100 down (the first is read as 100); each later phase adds a move, state, add or mechanic (NOTE otherwise); at least one
  weakness (NOTE); at least one far-band move or a gap closer (NOTE); `rebirth` only for legendary heroes; the removed
  fields of 12.10 (`damage_vs_players`, `defeat`, token and per-player fields) are read and ignored, each with its
  NOTE;
- equal treatment (6.15): no loaded hero can single out players. `target_is` kinds `player` and `npc` and the
  condition `target_is_player` read as `person`, `players_engaged` as `enemies_engaged`, `protects: players` as
  `allies`, `{player}` in fight lines as `{enemy}` (each ADJUSTED, with the owner's rule in the message); the
  `on_yield` trigger and the `yield` and `spared` line events are left out (PART LEFT OUT / NOTE, naming what
  replaces them);
- the end of a fight (6.13): `personality.when_losing` and `stands_for` words (the yield words refused); a NOTE when
  the authored traits flip the temperament's default kind; `stands_for` on a last-stand hero ignored with a NOTE;
  `flee_at` computed from `retreat_below` and the last phase and printed on the sheet; a NOTE on an escaping hero
  whose last phase starts under 18%; `on_escape` passives limited to self and look effects (an enemy effect is
  PART LEFT OUT);
- personality: every trait within ±20 of its temperament (ADJUSTED); leader: names unique across loaded heroes (NOTE),
  banner patterns from the vanilla list and never `mojang`, base style known, colours valid; talents known and at most
  one `realm`; voice ranges; lines' events and placeholders (v1 rules);
- across files: the uniqueness signature (archetype, element, style, ultimate form, signature move form and effect
  types) shared by two heroes is a NOTE naming both; relationships to unknown heroes are NOTEs.

### 14.2 Asset validation (`herogen check` and the Java `HeroAssetsTest`)

JSON syntax; geometry `format_version` 1.12.0; budgets (bones, cubes, animations, texture size); bone names from the
rig or declared by a part; required bones (`head`, `face`, `eyes`, `mouth`, `cheeks`, arms, legs, locators); face
planes on the fixed UV regions; texture size matches the geometry's `texture_width/height`; UV islands inside the
texture and not overlapping; every action code the server can send resolves through the hero's chain; `hit` markers
equal the server `frames` within one tick (G3: the timing files of 4.8 against the animation files, Python
`anim/quality.check_timing`); `face_ctrl` changes are jumps (pre/post keys, 4.6); joint limits, planted feet and
closed loops (13.6); action animations end on the idle settle key; whole-texel UVs; the alpha holes under the eye and
mouth planes and nothing in front of the face below head-local y 5.5, rotated corners included (4.6); at most 130 cubes
outside `fine_` bones (4.3); the eyes mirror unless the face is asymmetric by design (an eye patch, `iris2`, a
one-sided mark); `visual.json` move list equals the server move list;
every file in the chain exists; the build is reproducible (`build --check`); no two coplanar faces of different colour
(they z-fight in game) in the idle stance and the combat stance with the weapon drawn, in every phase, from six
directions at 6x (G6b: `HeroAssets.tie_report`, `check --no-ties` skips it).

Distinctness NOTEs (they feed review gate 2 with numbers instead of impressions): the preview renderer's front and side
silhouette masks of two heroes of the same archetype overlap more than 90% (intersection over union at 4x); the two
largest colour regions of two heroes of the same archetype are both within delta-E 15 of each other.

### 14.3 Unit tests

Java (`src/test/java/com/factionclash/logic/hero/` and `.../logic/leader/`):

| Test | Covers |
|---|---|
| `HeroJsonTest`, `HeroReaderTest`, `HeroBalanceTest`, `HeroRulesTest` | v1 §31.1 as designed there (format 1 still loads), with the equal readings of 6.15; `HeroRulesTest` also has the war power of 8.1 (the table's five rows, `war.heroPowerPercent`, the soldier-backbone cap, half power under half health) |
| `HeroReaderV2Test` | every new field, form, effect, condition, trigger; format-1-to-2 mapping; limits |
| `HeroBalanceV2Test` | class minimums, new weights and floors, combo cap, zone caps for rain; a stunning move raised to `heavy` whatever its form (6.4) |
| `MoveSelectorTest` | bands, novelty, posture, prefer, followups, reactions overriding, seeded picks; no move or reaction answer before the fight's opening gap (6.3) |
| `BossRulesTest` | boss health the same whoever and however many attack (the share kept when the maximum changes), the per-hit cap for every attacker with the excess to poise and none for damage without an attacker, the hurt cooldown per attacker (two attackers in one tick both land in full, one attacker twice within 10 ticks lands only its excess, damage with no attacker shares one cooldown: 6.2), poise factors by weapon, the parry window, stagger growth, enrage timing and the +25% ceiling; `equalForPlayerAndNpc`: every rule fed a player attacker and an NPC attacker with the same numbers gives the same result (the API takes no "is a player" input); nothing limits how many heroes press one target; `referenceDuel` (SLICE_PLAN.md criterion 15) |
| `EscapeRulesTest` (with `MOBILITY_AND_WORK.md` 17.1) | escape or last stand from the traits (the 30 temperament defaults of appendix A, the slice heroes, `sleepy`'s rule, the authored override, v1 `retreat_below` 0); the situation rules from loyalty and their end; `flee_at` (a third of `retreat_below`, 8-15, inside the last phase, the rounding); no escape inside the 10-minute window or with `heroes.escape` off: a last stand instead; cornered: it fights back and runs again once a way opens; the last-stand beat once per fight; `rebirth` before any escape |
| `HeroActionsTest` | `DATA_ACTION`, `DATA_STATE`, `DATA_MOOD` packing; code-to-name table |
| `HeroFaceRulesTest` | the frame choice of 4.6 as pure logic (`logic/hero/FaceRules`, used by the client's `HeroFace`): priority order, blink timing per profile and seed, no blink while bit 6 is set, the whole-number rule for `face_ctrl`, mood hold time |
| `PersonalityTest`, `TemperamentsTest` | `t_eff`, the ±20 rule, appendix A table |
| `LeaderBiasesTest` | every knob: neutral equals today's constant, monotonic, clamped, `k = 0` neutral; the worked numbers of 5.5 |
| `LeaderDraftTest` | seeded determinism, each rule, relaxation order, distinct `dye_primary` never relaxed, both genders, war balance |
| `FactionNameGenTest`, `PaletteTest`, `BannerDesignTest` | determinism, uniqueness, nearest dye and chat colour, no `mojang` |
| `TalentsTest` | tier numbers, strongest-copy stacking, realm rule |
| `BuiltinHeroesTest` | every shipped hero validates with no NOT LOADED and no PART LEFT OUT; ids unique; when the roster is complete, 100 female and 100 male |
| `HeroAssetsTest` | 14.2 over `src/main/resources` without Python |

Python: `tools/herogen/tests` (rig and bases, packer determinism, ramps, face frame rules, recipe expansion, secondary
bake, writer format, raster golden images) and `roster/check_roster.py`.

### 14.4 Review gates for every batch of heroes

1. All checks green (14.1-14.3), `build --check` clean.
2. **Contact sheet review** by a separate reviewer agent with a checklist, starting from the distinctness NOTEs of 14.2:
   distinct silhouette from every other hero of its archetype, palette readable in greyscale, face frames right (blink, talk, angry, pain), no clipping in
   `attack.1-3`, weapon held right in every style animation, phase looks visible, nothing that reads as an existing
   character (ROSTER_PLAN.md section 8).
3. **In-game screenshots**: the lineup front and back, and for each hero one wind-up with its decal, one phase
   transition and one face close-up; GIF previews of the signature moves.
4. **The owner's review** of the slice; for later batches see open question 3.
5. **A 3-day campaign smoke** with four leaders from the batch: no crash, no stuck hero (recoveries), every move cast
   at least once (`sim.py` hero lines), tick time within budget.

### 14.5 GameTests (milestone 1; each job-running test its own `batch`, test bases through `GameTestBases.register`, spawns at known relative positions on the flat test world)

Static (`workers_static`): `builtinHeroesLoad`, `heroSyncedFieldsDefaults`, `legacyIdentityUnchanged`,
`identityRenamesNameTagAndStation`.
Each its own batch: `heroMoveSetsActionAtWindup`, `heroHitLandsAfterWindupFrames`, `heroStunInterruptsWindup`,
`heroSpeedScalesFrames`, `staggerAtFullPoise`, `phaseTransitionAtThreshold`,
`telegraphDecalsGrowAndClean`, `comboStrikesTimed`, `chargeWallStagger`, `waveMissesJumpingTarget`,
`rainImpactsAfterDelay`, `sweepStopsAtBlocks`, `grabHoldsAndThrowsSafely` (never toward lava), `counterAbsorbsMelee`,
`barrageReaims`, `barrierBreaksAfterHits`, `decoyPopsAndHasNoShadow`, `guardBreakDisablesShield` (a mock player's
and an NPC soldier's raised shield, the same seconds), `callGuardsRetargets`, `leaderSpawnsAtStation`,
`leaderCourtDutyStaysHome`, `leaderRevivesAtCapital`, `leaderBiasesChangeRaidInterval`,
`talentSurveyorWidensMineSearch`, `talentAnglerWidensFishing`, `talentMendHealsAllies`, `heroWorksItsDuty` (a
miner-duty hero mines), `heroKitNeverDrops`, `heroNotDraftedIntoRaidByDefault` (3.2).

Equal treatment, no tokens and no yield (the owner's decisions of 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13; these twelve replace the
first draft's `bossModeOnPlayerEngage`, `bossModeEndsAfterLeaving`, `bossDamageCapToPoise`, `yieldThenSpare`,
`yieldThenFinish` and `attackTokensLimitPressers`; each its own batch):

- `heroHasBossHealthAgainstAnyone`: a fixture hero has its boss maximum from spawn, the same with nobody near, with an
  NPC soldier engaged, with a mock player engaged and with both; a second attacker changes nothing.
- `bossBarShownInAnyFightNearAPlayer`: a hero fighting an NPC soldier shows its bar and intro title to a mock player
  within 32 blocks and to nobody when no player is near; the fight itself (frames, damage) is the same in both.
- `fightEndsTenSecondsAfterLastEnemy`: the bar goes 10 s after the last engaged enemy, NPC or player, is gone;
  healing starts after 30 s; a phase re-arms 10% above its threshold.
- `hitCapHoldsForNpcAndPlayerHits`: an oversized blow from an NPC and the same blow from a mock player each take 12%
  of the hero's maximum and put the excess into poise; lava is not capped. A third run has two NPC soldiers and a
  mock player strike in the same tick: all three hits land in full (one hurt cooldown per attacker, 6.2).
- `reactionFiresOnNpcShield`: an NPC soldier raising a shield draws the hero's `target_blocking` answer with the same
  chance and cooldown as a mock player raising one (the same seed, the same picks).
- `stunStopsPlayerLikeNpc`: a hero's stun stops a mock player's movement, attack and item use for its seconds, as
  `StunnedGoal` stops an NPC (6.15); a second hero's stun inside the immunity window does nothing to either (6.14).
- `threeHeroesPressOneTarget`: three hostile heroes are all in melee on one NPC soldier at once, and in a second run
  on one mock player; none circles or waits for a turn, and no move hits either target before the opening gap plus
  its wind-up (6.3).
- `escapingHeroFleesAtThresholdFromNpcsAndPlayer`: an escaping-kind fixture (schemer traits) brought under its
  `flee_at` by NPC soldiers breaks off, the bar shows `fled`, nothing drops; the same fixture against a mock player
  does the same at the same health.
- `lastStandHeroNeverFleesAndDiesFighting`: a last-stand fixture (battle maniac traits) at 5% health is still
  attacking, says its `last_stand` line once, dies at zero with no kneel and no cancelled blow, the bar shows
  `fallen`, and its revival timer starts; killed by NPCs or by a mock player alike.
- `homeStandHeroFleesAfieldNotAtHome`: an escaping-kind fixture with loyalty 80 runs from a fight outside its test
  base and fights to the death inside it; with the heart destroyed it runs.
- `rewardsDropOnlyOnDeath`: XP orbs, the crest and non-kit gear drop where a hero died, for an NPC killer and for a
  mock player; an escaped hero drops nothing; soldiers of the winning side pick the orbs up.
- `warCouncilCountsHeroPower` (3.2, 8.1): a base's defence with its hero home, on a raid, away after an escape and
  dead; the soldier-backbone cap.

One GameTest per remaining milestone-1 talent, so every live talent has its effect tested in the world (their tier
numbers and the strongest-copy rule are in the unit test `TalentsTest`): `talentAuraAddsAffixStack`,
`talentEmberKeeperFasterSmelting`, `talentRallyCheaperRecruit`, `talentMentorBoostsWorkXp`, `talentDrillSoldierXp`,
`talentPurifierClearsPoison`, `talentLookoutAlarmRadius`, `talentCartographerScoutReach`,
