  `onArrowShot`; `NpcMeleeAttackGoal` stands aside for a thrown hero; `ModEntities` `hero_projectile` and `hero_zone`
  with vanilla renderers; `HeroEntity.speak` for a move's own line; `build.gradle` passes the slice fixtures' folder
  to the GameTest run (`-Dfactionclash.sliceFixtures`). New config `[heroes]` `powerScale`, `maxHit`,
  `maxDotPerSecond` (`docs/factionclash-common.toml` regenerated from a fresh server).
- **Which slice moves run now**: every move of the eight fixtures whose form and effects are all A3's (Natsuho: claw
  string, pounce, hiss, scratch dash); the counter, charge, wave, rain, sweep, grab and barrage forms, the stun,
  taunt, dispel, empower and summon effects and the format-2 effects wait for A4 and A5, and their moves are not
  picked until then (the list: kit-gaps "A3 decisions").
- **Tests**: `AbilityRuntimeTest` (12), `FormGeometryTest` (9), `MovePlanTest` (8) and `StatusBookTest` (12): 877 unit
  tests. `HeroAbilityGameTests` (23): `heroSkillDamagesEnemy`, `heroUltimateAtFullEnergy`,
  `cooldownsAndEnergySurviveASave`, `cooldownCutAndEnergyEffects`, `heroNovaSparesAlliesAndAnimals`,
  `chainJumpsBetweenEnemies`, `coneAndLineReachOnlyTheirShape`, `heroZonePulses`, `heroProjectileLeavesNoFire`,
  `heroLeapMovesPhysically`, `heroDashMovesAndHitsAlongItsPath`, `heroHealsHurtAlly`,
  `heroShieldExpiresOnNpcAndPlayerAlike`, `heroDotTicksThroughIframes`, `heroElementImmunity`, `heroMarksStackAndSpend`,
  `cleanseRemovesTheHarmfulPart`, `natsuhoComboHitsSoldierAndPlayerEqually` (the slice fixture's claw string, strike by
  strike, and its bleed), `knockbackAndPullTheSameForNpcAndPlayer`, `statAndEffectTheSameForNpcAndPlayer`,
  `slotCapHoldsForAnyTarget`, `thrownHeroThrowsAndGainsEnergy` and `bowHeroArrowsAreBasicHits`: 183 GameTests, all
  passing. Sabotage checks, each caught: six in the pure rules (no slot cap, a restored recharge not clamped, DoTs
  twice a second, a knock-up over a block, chain jumps 4 ticks apart, every form runnable) and 22 in the game that
  between them fail all 23 GameTests (half damage on a player, caught by the Natsuho and the slot-cap tests; no
  cooldowns saved; every faction NPC an enemy, which in a shared run also hid three of the others, so those were run
  again alone; a shield never taken away, the hurt cooldown not put back, a thrown hit or an arrow that is no basic hit,
  one zone pulse too many, half the knock-back on a player, an ultimate that spends nothing, no undead nature, a
  cleanse that leaves the fire, the least hurt ally healed, `clear_marks` and `cooldown_cut` doing nothing, a chain
  that jumps back, stats skipping players, a cone of any angle, no opening gap, a leap that lands at take-off, a dash
  three times as fast, a projectile that sets fire).
- **Left open**: stun, taunt, dispel, empower, summon and the passives, `on_mark_full` among them (A4); the format-2
  forms and effects, the timing files' frames, `DATA_ACTION`, speed and interrupts (A5); the per-hit boss cap,
  poise, phases and decals (A6); custom projectile and zone looks (A9); the station sheet's moves. Not yet checked on a
  dedicated server in a campaign: only the test rig ships, and its one move (a dash) runs now.

### 15.4 Milestones 3-10: content batches (ROSTER_PLAN.md section 6)

Each batch k (24 heroes): **Ck.1-Ck.6** author, generate and check 4 heroes each (M each; a hero is a 15-25 KB source
file with a kit, 10-16 animation recipes and 40-60 lines, so 4 per session is the realistic size: the slice tasks do 2;
depends on G8 for styles and parts the batch uses, and on A8/A9 when its heroes use adds or custom projectiles);
**Ck.7** clearance and review gates of 14.4 with the contact sheet and in-game screenshots (S); **Ck.8** fixes and a
3-day campaign smoke (S). Batch 1
can start after milestone 1 using the slice's libraries plus the styles it needs; milestone 3's player-side systems
(wanderers, recruitment, duels, escort, commissions, bonds, posts, awakened ultimates: tasks L11-L14) run in parallel
with batches 1-3.

### 15.5 Final milestone

Balance pass from cast, damage and fight-length statistics across all 200; performance with all 200 loaded (heap,
reload time, a 64-hero lineup); the 30-day soak; adversarial review; docs; release.

### 15.6 Implementation notes (equal treatment, no attack tokens, no yield)

The owner's decisions of 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-13 (section 6) as work for the Java tasks. They change no task's
size; each line names the task that carries it. One test of all of it: no method of `logic/hero/` takes "is a
player" as an input, and every GameTest that names "an NPC soldier and a mock player" gives the same numbers for
both (14.5).

**Format and validation (F1, F2: `HeroCatalogue`, `HeroReader`, `HeroDef`)**

- Already in F1 as built (15.2 "F1 as built"): `target_is` kinds `player` and `npc` read as `person`,
  `protects: players` as `allies` (ADJUSTED, with the owner's rule in the message).
- Add: the condition `target_is_player` reads as `target_is` `person` and `players_engaged` as `enemies_engaged`;
  `{player}` in fight lines (`intro`) reads as `{enemy}`; `boss.damage_vs_players`, `boss.defeat` and any token or
  per-player field are read and ignored, each with the NOTE of 12.10; the `on_yield` trigger is left out and
  `on_escape` and `on_last_stand` exist (6.6); the line events `yield` and `spared` are left out with a NOTE and
  `escape` and `last_stand` exist (12.15); `personality.when_losing` and `stands_for` with their synonyms, the
  refusal of the yield words, the NOTE when traits flip the temperament's default and the NOTE on a late last phase
  (12.11, 14.1).
- `HeroDef`: the personality carries `whenLosing` (authored or absent) and `standsFor` (authored or absent); nothing
  in `HeroDef` names a yield, a spare or a player multiplier.

- Done in F2 (15.2 "F2 as built"): all of the above, with a test that no `HeroDef` component, enum constant or word
  names a player, an NPC, a yield, a spare or a token.

**`HeroBalance` (F1, F2)**

- The slot caps (skill 16, ultimate 30), `heroes.maxHit` and the cooldown floors are checked against the definition,
  so they hold against any target. No budget has an "against players" column: `damage_vs_players` is not an input,
  and no cap is relaxed or tightened by the kind of the receiver.
- Control caps are the same for every receiver (stun 2 s per skill, 3 per ultimate, `guard_break` 2-5 s, grabs 1 s).
  Where a budget weighs a stun as milder because v1 gave players Slowness instead, that weight goes: a stun now
  stops a player (6.15). For the same reason a move with a `stun` effect on enemies is raised to at least `heavy`
  whatever its form (ADJUSTED, 6.4); the class is resolved before the frames are clamped, so the 0.8 s minimum
  applies.
- It computes and prints `flee_at` (6.13) with the kind ("escapes under 15%, never from its home" / "fights to the
  death") on the validator's sheet, next to the level table of v1 §22.2.

**`HeroRules` (F1)**

- Damage: one path for every target (it is so already); `targetIsBoss` is true for a hero, so
  `target_max_health_percent` counts at most 2% against one (6.2).
- Done in F4 (15.2 "F4 as built"): the war power below; the rewards came with F3.
- War power: `heroPower(maxHealth, level, percent)` is the formula of 8.1 and replaces the provisional body;
  `HERO_DEFENDERS` and `WAR_POWER_IS_PROVISIONAL` go; add `countedPower(soldiersPower, heroesPower)` (the
  soldier-backbone cap: heroes at most 1.5 times the soldiers) and the half weight under half health;
  `HeroRulesTest.warPowerIsAHookUntilTheDesignLands` becomes the five rows of 8.1's table and the cap.
- Rewards: `rewardXp(level)` = min(100, 7 x level); the crest key is (hero, winning side, in-game day), a side being
  a faction (a member player counts as its faction) or a factionless player. Nothing here knows of a yield or a spare.

**`BossRules` (F3)**

- Done in F3 (15.2 "F3 as built"): every line below, with the fight-reach clock, air poise and the rewards.

- `maxHealth(baseMax, healthScale, roleFactor)`: no player count, no attacker count.
- `capHit(amount, maxHealth, share)` returns what lands and what goes to poise, for a hit with any attacking entity;
  damage with no attacker is not passed through it.
- `hurtCooldown(lastTick, lastAmount, now, amount)`: vanilla's rule for one source (within 10 ticks of that source's
  last full hit only the excess over its amount lands; after that a full hit), so the game side can keep it per
  attacker (6.2). The key is the attacking entity (`DamageSource.getEntity()`: for an arrow its shooter, so an
  archer's arrows and blows share one key), one shared key for all damage with no attacker.
- Poise by weapon kind, the parry by `isBlocking` and the ticks the shield has been up, whoever holds it; stagger
  growth; the enrage clock on fight seconds.
- Not to be built: attack tokens, the no-one-shot rule, `damageVsPlayers`, health per engaged player, and the
  interrupted draft's `BossRules.spares` (an NPC leader deciding to spare): there is nothing to spare.
- The fight is a set of engaged enemies of any kind and a clock that ends 10 s after the last leaves (6.1).

**`MoveSelector` (F3)**

- Done in F3 (15.2 "F3 as built"): every line below; the conditions read `Sense`, which holds the `TargetState`.

- One loop in every fight: no field-mode branch (the phase gap is never replaced by the global cooldown). The gap
  timer starts when the fight starts (or when the intro ends), so the first pick, reaction answers included, waits a
  full phase-1 gap (6.3).
- Conditions read one `TargetState` (blocking, drawing a bow, eating, airborne, above, behind, far, unreachable)
  filled the same way from a player and from an NPC; it has no "is a player" field, and reactions use the same
  chance and cooldown for both.
- No token check: a melee or heavy move is never filtered by how many heroes already press the target.
- It is not asked for a move while the hero escapes; a cornered hero asks again.

**`EscapeRules` (F5, with `MOBILITY_AND_WORK.md` 18.1)**

- Done in F3 (15.2 "F3 as built"): `kind`, `standsFor` and `fleeAt` (F2) and the runtime decision below, as
  `lowHealth`, `lostRaid` and `lostBase`; cornered, the refuge and the stay away beside them. Done in F5 (15.2 "F5
  as built"): `decide` over the three, the scout's step aside, pursuers, the wild, no rewards, morale, the war council.

- `kind(traits, temperament, authored, retreatBelow)`: escape or last stand (nerve 65, `sleepy`, the override, v1
  `retreat_below` 0); `standsFor(loyalty, authored)`; `fleeAt(retreatBelow, lastPhaseAt)`.
- `decide(...)`: under `flee_at` in any fight (the enemy's kind is not an input), a lost raid, a lost base; no
  escape inside the 10-minute window, held by a situation, with an unspent `rebirth`, or with `heroes.escape` off:
  then the last-stand beat, once per fight. Called after every hit that lands on the hero and once a tick. Cornered
  is not a decision: `HeroEscapeGoal` lets the fight goals run while no way away is open (3 s) and takes over again
  when one opens (6.13).
- The first draft's "refused yield" trigger (honour 30, a cautious leader) and "a leader saving itself" are not
  built.

**Game side (A3-A6, A12, L3, L4)**

- `BossController` (A6): the health modifier is permanent (spawn, revival, load); the hit cap sits in the hero's
  incoming-damage hook for any entity source; **no code path cancels lethal damage**; bar, intro title and decals go
  to players within 32 blocks and decide nothing; the bar's `fled` and `fallen` states; rewards at death to the
  winning side, with soldiers picking up orbs and the crest.
- `HeroEntity.hurt` (A6): the hurt cooldown per attacker of 6.2. Before `super.hurt`, unless the source is a hero
  damage type (which `HeroDamage` already clears and restores, v1 §6.3), set `invulnerableTime` and `lastHurt` from
  that source's own record in a small transient map (key as `BossRules.hurtCooldown`; `20 - elapsed` and its amount
  inside 10 ticks, else 0 and 0); after it, store the source's tick and amount when the hit landed in full, or the
  new amount when it landed an excess. Entries older than 10 ticks are dropped; the map is not saved.
- The start of an escape (A6, A12): `EscapeRules.decide` runs from the hero's post-damage hook and its tick; an
  escape cancels a move in its wind-up through the same interrupt a stun uses (`heroStunInterruptsWindup`'s path:
  decals removed, no hit), ends a string, and lets a move in its active part finish that tick (6.13).
- Goals (A6, A12): `HeroEscapeGoal` at priority 1, no `HeroYieldGoal`; `HeroEntity` does not register the soldiers'
  `WithdrawGoal` (done in the review of F1-F5 and A1b: `FactionNpc.withdrawsToRecover`, false for a hero; A1a and A1b
  had left it on, so a hero with boss health stepped back to eat under 30%); `HeroTargetGoal` has no token and drops an
  escaping hero when its own honour is 60 or more.
- `HeroStatus` (A4): a stun holds a player (the speed and jump modifier, the cancelled attack, use, place and break
  events), a taunt halves every receiver's damage to others, blindness and darkness cut an NPC's ranges, a
  `guard_break` keeps an NPC's shield down (6.15).
- `HeroActions` (as built in A1a; the rename is done in F3): bit 7 of `DATA_STATE` is renamed from `STATE_YIELDING` to
  `STATE_ESCAPING` (same bit, `HeroActionsTest` updated); codes 21 and 22 keep their numbers and clip names until the
  art follow-up below
  is decided. `FaceRules`' first rule ("dead or defeated: eyes closed") reads only "dead"; an escaping hero's face is
  its mood byte.
- Done in A1b (15.2 "A1b as built"): `soldierPower` over ordinary soldiers, the defence power with the home heroes'
  war power and the backbone cap, the target score in soldier units, `war.heroPowerPercent`, the council line for
  `sim.py`; the party's heroes, the escape's away time and the leader rules wait for L4/A12.
- `FactionDirector` (L3): `soldierPower` averages ordinary soldiers only; the party's power adds the heroes that go;
  `defenders` becomes a defence power (soldiers at home x `soldierPower` + the home heroes' war power, half under
  half health, none while away or dead) and the target score uses it in soldier units; the soldier-backbone cap on
  both sides; the fall-back share stays in heads; a leader's escape ends its raid by the rule of 5.6; the vengeance
  raid after a leader's death goes through the normal attack-odds check, with the dead leader counted out (5.6).
- `HeroState`: `escapes` and `lastStands`; no `yields`, `spares`, `restUntil` or `spareCooldowns`.
- Commands and tools: `/fc admin hero escape <id>` replaces the `yield` hook (12.1); `sim.py` hero lines count
  fights, staggers, escapes, last stands, deaths and revivals, and print the war power.
- Config: no `boss.attackTokens`, no `boss.damageVsPlayers`; `war.heroPowerPercent`; `heroes.escape`,
  `escapeAwaySeconds`, `escapeCooldownSeconds` (`MOBILITY_AND_WORK.md` 8.5).

**Art and sound follow-ups (for the art and sound work; nothing was edited by this pass)**

- Animations (`notes/artgen.md`, G4): the core clips `defeated`, `defeated.loop` and `spared` (action codes 21 and
  22, with a restyled copy per style library) were the yield's kneel and the rise of a spared hero. No hero yields
  now. Proposed reuse rather than deletion, so the codes do not move: the kneel and its loop as the **wounded rest**
  of an escaped hero at its refuge (it stays there 5 minutes, hurt), and `spared` as its **rise** when it goes back
  to its duty; a rename (`rest.wounded`, `rest.wounded.loop`, `rest.rise`) can come with any later rebuild of
  `_core`. If the reuse is not wanted they are retired: removed from `_core`, the restyled copies and
  `anim/core_preview.py`, with codes 21 and 22 marked reserved.
- Sounds (`SOUND.md` 16): `cues.yield` (the chime "Hero yields") is reused as the bar's **fled** sting (proposed id
  `cues.fled`, subtitle "Hero flees"); `cues.spared` ("Hero is spared") is retired, or reused as the chime of a
  hero's return at its revival; a **fallen** sting for the bar's other end state and a **last stand** accent are
  new, generated or vanilla by id.
- Lines (C1-C4): each slice hero's `yield` and `spared` lines are replaced by an `escape` line, a `last_stand` line
  or both, and a `fallen` line (SLICE_PLAN.md section 3).

## 16. Risks and mitigations

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| The GeckoLib host is blocked again | no animated models | it answered on 2026-11-12 (4.2); otherwise the owner approves the host once (A0); the jar is then vendored and builds never need it again; the hand-written fallback (4.1) is the plan B |
| Art quality across 200 generated heroes (the biggest real risk) | samey or ugly heroes | a large parts library, archetype templates, hue-shifted painting, HD faces, per-hero signature animations, contact-sheet review per batch, in-game screenshots, the owner's review of the slice before scaling up, Blockbench-editable outputs for later hand polish |
| A hero reads as an existing character | originality broken | uniqueness rules in the roster checker and the validator, name and concept searches, a reviewer pass and the owner's review (ROSTER_PLAN.md section 8) |
| Client cost with many heroes on screen | frame drops in big wars | cube budget, LOD, culling, measurements after the slice and batches 4 and 8; most scenes have a few heroes |
| Client memory for 200 baked models | larger heap | measured; lazy baking if over 100 MB |
| Animation and server timings drift | unfair hits | one recipe writes both; `HeroAssetsTest` fails on a difference |
| Boss fights feel unfair or too easy | bad fights | class minimums, fairness rules enforced in code and the same for everyone, PROPOSED numbers tuned from the slice campaign and the owner's play session |
| Heroes decide NPC wars by themselves, now that they have boss health against soldiers | one hero makes or breaks every raid | the war power and its limits (8.1: the soldier-backbone cap, numbers adding up against a hero through one hurt cooldown per attacker, escapes and last stands, the 10-minute revival); `war.heroPowerPercent`; `sim.py` counts raids decided by a hero's presence |
| Several heroes on one player (no attack tokens) | a fight a player in iron cannot win in the open | the owner accepts it (2026-11-13); every hero waits its opening gap and every move its wind-up, stunning moves are heavy and stuns cannot chain (6.3, 6.4, 6.14); top gear, choke points and leaving the heroes' leash are the answers (6.16) |
| Escapes frustrate (five of the eight slice heroes can run) | a fight with no ending and no reward | one escape in 10 minutes and a last stand inside that window, a stagger or a shot-down flyer stops it, the hero is found hurt at its refuge, two of the five never run from their own base; `heroes.escape = false` makes every hero fight to the death |
| Personality makes campaigns end early or stall | bad campaigns | draft war-balance rule, `personalityStrength`, campaign checks comparing personalities |
| Refactoring `FactionType` reads breaks something | regressions | M1 is mechanical and keeps every test unchanged; slots and identity are separate steps |
| A future Minecraft upgrade | GeckoLib 5 rewrite | assets are Bedrock files and stay; only the renderer and model glue (about 600-900 lines) change |
| Scope (200 heroes with complex kits) | very long project | the slice proves every piece first; batches are independent and can ship one by one |

## 17. Open questions for the owner

1. **The look.** The heroes will be Minecraft-style models made of cubes with pixel-art textures: anime proportions,
   detailed hair, ears, tails, horns, wings and outfits, and large expressive faces that blink, talk and change
   expression. They will not be smooth high-detail anime models (Minecraft cannot draw those well, and they would
   look out of place). Is this the look you want? The slice screenshots will show it before anything is scaled up.
2. **Your own heroes.** Earlier you planned to write your own heroes with a chatbot and keep them in a private
   repository. With original heroes made by Claude, the design drops the "your own hero JSON plus a 64x64 skin" path
   (operators can still override a built-in hero's kit in the config folder). Do you still want your own heroes
   installable in some form, for example on the old player-model look without animations? *Answered on 2026-11-12*:
   not needed. Only the built-in heroes; operators can still override a built-in hero's name or kit in the config
   folder. (Questions 1 and 3 were answered the same day: the look is approved; each batch of 24 waits for the
   owner's review.)
3. **Review cadence.** After you approve the 8-hero slice from screenshots, should each batch of 24 wait for your
   review of its contact sheet and screenshots, or should Claude continue through the batches and send you the
   galleries as they are done?
4. **Network permission.** Building this needs GeckoLib from `dl.cloudsmith.io`. That host was refused on 2026-11-11
   and answered on 2026-11-12, so you may see no prompt at all; if it is refused again when the work starts, you will
   see a prompt to allow it once. After that the library is stored in the repository and the host is no longer needed.
   (An action for you, not a design choice.)
5. **Mobility and work mastery.** *Answered on 2026-11-12* (`MOBILITY_AND_WORK.md` 19.1): heroes may use their
   abilities to make things "unfair" (they cross hostile walls and reach players anywhere), the 27 flyers stay, every
   hero has all of its abilities from level 1 but not at full power (applied to moves, phases, ultimates and talents
   too: section 8), and an escaping hero may make pursuit impossible. The size of the extra output was not answered:
   those numbers stay and are tuned after you play. Later the same day the owner confirmed: keep the extra output
   sizes (tuned after play), and keep a common or rare hero's third phase and the awakened second ultimate from level 1.

6. **Duels (milestone 3; a design point, not a question that blocks the slice).** Heroes no longer yield (your
   decision of 2026-11-13), and a yield was how sparring, duels and "beat it in a duel" recruitment ended. Those need
   a formal non-lethal bout instead: both sides agree before the first blow, and it ends at a set point (PROPOSED:
   first to half health, as Kagenori's dawn sparring with soldiers already does; nobody dies, nothing drops). An
   agreed bout is not a yield in a real fight: a hero attacked in earnest still escapes or fights to the death. Left
   open until milestone 3: who may call a bout and where (the ring of decals), what breaks it off (a third party's
   blow, an alarm), and what winning gives (a wanderer joins, a delinquent's respect, a hot-blooded hero's word).

## Appendix A: temperaments (defaults, 0-100)

a aggression, h honour, m ambition, c caution, g greed, s sociability, l loyalty, v volatility. Focus is the default
`economy_focus`; Recruit the default wanderer condition; Admires and Clashes give the affinity used in opinion and
compatibility (±20 / ±0.2).

| Temperament | a | h | m | c | g | s | l | v | Special rule | Focus | Recruit | Admires / clashes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| hot_blooded | 80 | 70 | 65 | 20 | 30 | 65 | 75 | 55 | always accepts duels; vengeance raids even when a < 70 | military | duel | stoic / schemer |
| stoic | 50 | 80 | 45 | 65 | 30 | 30 | 85 | 10 | never surprise-breaks (as h >= 75) | mining | reputation | gruff_veteran / airhead |
| cool_headed | 45 | 70 | 55 | 70 | 35 | 35 | 70 | 15 | opinion weighs half in pact maths | craft | reputation | ancient_sage / genki |
| tsundere | 65 | 65 | 60 | 45 | 40 | 45 | 70 | 45 | refuses the first pact offer from each faction | military | duel | hot_blooded / charmer |
| shy | 25 | 70 | 30 | 80 | 30 | 35 | 80 | 20 | seeks a pact with the strongest known neighbour | farming | visits | big_sibling / delinquent |
| sweet_natured | 25 | 75 | 40 | 50 | 25 | 90 | 80 | 35 | offers peace to every faction at first contact | food | gift | gentle_giant / tyrant |
| yandere | 70 | 40 | 55 | 35 | 35 | 40 | 95 | 60 | hard enrage when its favoured is hurt; never forgives its top grudge | military | cause | big_sibling / charmer |
| genki | 70 | 60 | 75 | 25 | 35 | 85 | 65 | 80 | builds ahead (+1 bed threshold); impulsive raids | growth | gift | charmer / sleepy |
| lone_wolf | 55 | 60 | 40 | 60 | 40 | 10 | 40 | 30 | never proposes pacts; accepts only when T >= 2 | scouting | duel | stoic / sweet_natured |
| proud_noble | 55 | 70 | 80 | 50 | 60 | 50 | 60 | 30 | opinion losses x1.5; builds monuments | trade | reputation | righteous / delinquent |
| delinquent | 75 | 55 | 55 | 30 | 45 | 55 | 85 | 55 | territorial; respects whoever beats its leader in a duel | military | duel | hot_blooded / proud_noble |
| chuunibyou | 65 | 60 | 80 | 30 | 30 | 60 | 65 | 75 | opens boss fights with its ultimate when ready; grand raid names | military | duel | eccentric_genius / cool_headed |
| airhead | 35 | 60 | 40 | 40 | 30 | 75 | 60 | 95 | doubled noise; the luck whim | food | gift | sweet_natured / perfectionist |

Special rules that need milestone 2 systems (pacts, morale, duels) stay inactive in milestone 1; the trait values work
from the start.

**When a fight goes badly** (6.13; the defaults that follow from the table above, and what `personality.when_losing`
and `stands_for` override). Nerve is a + h - c: 65 or more is a last stand. An escaping temperament still stands at
its home base with loyalty 70 or more, and beside its fighting leader with 85 or more.

## Appendix B: base style families

Wall, frame, floor, roof (and stairs), perimeter, seat noun. Accents (wool, carpet, banners, glass) come from the
identity colours. Blocks marked * are added to `MaterialRules.STONE_BLOCKS` so repairs can substitute. Milestone 1
builds the four legacy families plus the six marked M1.

| Family | Wall | Frame | Floor | Roof (stairs) | Perimeter | Seat | Milestone |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ember_fortress` (legacy Ember) | bricks | dark oak log | dark oak planks | deepslate tiles | polished blackstone bricks | Keep | legacy |
| `grove_lodge` (legacy Grove) | oak planks | oak log | spruce planks | spruce planks | mossy stone bricks | Lodge | legacy |
| `tide_port` (legacy Tide) | prismarine bricks | birch log | birch planks | dark prismarine | smooth sandstone | Port | legacy |
| `stone_hold` (legacy Stoneguard) | stone bricks | spruce log | spruce planks | cobblestone (stone brick stairs) | stone bricks | Hold | legacy |
| `shrine_pavilion` | birch planks | stripped mangrove log | spruce planks | deepslate tiles | stone bricks | Shrine | M1 |
| `cherry_court` | cherry planks | stripped cherry log | birch planks | deepslate tiles | smooth stone* | Court | M1 |
| `bamboo_village` | bamboo planks | jungle log | bamboo planks | spruce planks | cobblestone | Village | M1 |
| `frost_hall` | polished diorite* | spruce log | spruce planks | dark prismarine | stone bricks | Hall | M1 |
| `dark_manor` | polished blackstone bricks | dark oak log | dark oak planks | deepslate tiles | blackstone | Manor | M1 |
| `deepslate_keep` | deepslate bricks | dark oak log | spruce planks | deepslate tiles | cobbled deepslate | Keep | M1 |
| `desert_court` | smooth sandstone | acacia log | cut sandstone* | smooth sandstone (sandstone stairs) | sandstone | Court | M2 |
| `ivory_palace` | quartz bricks* | birch log | birch planks | smooth stone* | polished diorite* | Palace | M2 |
| `jungle_haven` | jungle planks | jungle log | jungle planks | mossy cobblestone | mossy stone bricks | Haven | M2 |
| `tuff_workshop` | tuff bricks* | spruce log | spruce planks | deepslate tiles | polished tuff* | Workshop | M2 |
| `mud_town` | mud bricks* | acacia log | acacia planks | mud bricks* | red sandstone* | Town | M2 |
| `coral_port` | dark prismarine | mangrove log | mangrove planks | prismarine bricks | sandstone | Port | M2 |

Until a family exists, a leader whose style is not built yet uses the nearest built family by material colour (the
validator prints which).

## Appendix C: archetype defaults (summary)

Each `herosrc/archetypes/<archetype>.json` holds: default base and build by gender, default parts (ears, horns, tails,
wings, signature headwear), palette presets, the default style library, the default base style (ROSTER_PLAN.md
section 2.3), the banner emblem (beast-kin: `triangles_top` + `circle`; shrine and priests: `straight_cross` +
`stripe_center`; knights and paladins: `cross` + `border`; plants: `flower` + `gradient_up`; undead and dark: `skull` +
`curly_border`; storm and wind: `stripe_downleft` + `gradient`; sea: `flow`; craft and tech: `bricks` +
`square_top_left`; stars and idols: `rhombus` + `circle`; dragons: `triangles_bottom` + `gradient`), naming motif words
for the faction name generator, the folklore weak point it suggests, and 2-3 kit patterns from
`research-archetypes.md` part 4.

## Appendix D: action codes (`DATA_ACTION` bits 0-7)

| Codes | Names |
|---|---|
| 0 | none (controller returns to `base`) |
| 1-4 | `attack.1`, `attack.2`, `attack.3`, `attack.ranged` |
| 5-11 | `guard` (+`.loop`), `guard.hit`, `dodge`, `stagger`, `stun` (+`.loop`), `interrupt`, `hurt.heavy` |
| 12-15 | `draw`, `sheathe`, `taunt`, `eat` |
| 16-23 | `intro`, `phase.2`, `phase.3`, `enrage`, `victory`, `defeated` (+`.loop`), `spared`, `founding` |
| 24-26 | `state.<1..3>` (names from `visual.json`) |
| 32-47 | `work.mine`, `work.chop`, `work.till`, `work.plant`, `work.harvest`, `work.fish_cast`, `work.fish_wait` (+`.loop`), `work.fish_reel`, `work.cook`, `work.smith`, `work.build`, `work.read`, `work.pray`, `work.sweep`, `work.tend`, `work.eat` |
| 48-63 | `emote.greet`, `emote.bow`, `emote.wave`, `emote.laugh`, `emote.angry`, `emote.sad`, `emote.think`, `emote.cheer`, `emote.talk`, `emote.shrug`, `personal.1-3` (rare idles started by the server for synchronised moments) |
| 64-75 | `move.<id>` for move slots 0-11 (names from `visual.json`, in the server's move order) |
| 76-79 | `ultimate`, `ultimate.end` (`ultimate.loop` plays automatically when it exists), `ultimate.awakened`, reserved |
| 80-255 | reserved |

Codes 21 and 22 (`defeated` with its loop, `spared`) were the yield's kneel and the rise of a spared hero. No hero
yields (6.13), so the server no longer sends them at the end of a fight; the clips and the codes stay as built until
the art follow-up of 15.6 decides between reusing them (the wounded rest of an escaped hero and its rise) and
retiring them.
